# categorification-lean

Lean 4 formalizations of results in categorification. Depends on Mathlib,
[string-diagrams-lean](https://github.com/apellis/string-diagrams-lean),
[lie-lean](https://github.com/apellis/lie-lean) and
[dg-lean](https://github.com/apellis/dg-lean).

Errors found in the sources while formalizing them are listed in [ERRATA](ERRATA.md).

## Contents

### Headline: Khovanov–Lauda I, Theorem 1.1

For a simple graph `Γ` (arbitrary, not necessarily finite) and a field `𝕜`, the map
`γ : _A f → K_0(R)` sending `θ_{i_1}^{(a_1)} ⋯ θ_{i_r}^{(a_r)}` to the class of the projective
`P_{i_1^{(a_1)} ⋯ i_r^{(a_r)}}` is a ring isomorphism, `ℤ[q, q⁻¹]`-linear (with `q` acting on
`_A f` as `v⁻¹`): `KLR.KLGamma.gammaIntEquiv`, `KLR.KLGamma.theorem_1_1`,
`KLR.KLGamma.gammaIntEquiv_dpowMono`, `KLR.KLGamma.gammaIntEquiv_smul`; over `ℚ(v)`,
`KLR.KLGamma.gammaFEquiv` (Prop. 3.18). The theorems are stated with the quantum Gabber–Kac
theorem (Lusztig, Thm. 33.1.3: the radical of the form on `'f` is generated by the quantum Serre
relations) as an explicit argument `QuantumGroup.PreF.GabberKac`, used only to define `γ` on `f`
(the kernel of `'f → K_0(R)` is shown to lie in the radical unconditionally). All uses of
Gabber–Kac in the library (KL I, KL II, KL III Prop. 2.5) follow from a single statement in
Lusztig's normalization, `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.QuantumGabberKac` (over `ℚ(v)`, the radical of
`(θ_i, θ_i) = (1 - v_i^{-2})^{-1}` equals the ideal generated by the quantum Serre elements), via
transport along field automorphisms and rescaling of the form. **This statement is proved for
every Cartan datum** (`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.quantumGabberKac`), by comparison with the quantum
Gabber–Kac theorem of [lie-lean](https://github.com/apellis/lie-lean) (`LusztigF.radical_eq_serreIdeal_ratFunc`,
finitely many simple roots) and a reduction from arbitrary index sets to finite ones, so the
headline theorems hold unconditionally: `KLR.KLGamma.theorem_1_1_unconditional`,
`KLR.KLGamma.gammaIntEquiv_unconditional`, `KLR.KL2Gamma.theorem_8_unconditional`,
`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5_unconditional`. General finite-Cartan nondegeneracy
is proved over every field by `KL3.Diagram.calculusNondeg`, and
`KL3.Diagram.canonicalBasis` gives the KL diagram basis over every commutative ring.
Thus KL III Thm. 1.2 has no remaining nondegeneracy premise in
`gammaUA'_bijective_unconditional` and `gammaAlg_injective_unconditional`;
`gammaAlgEquiv_unconditional` supplies the categorification equivalence.
Along the way:
Prop. 3.20 in a form that does not need `Γ` finite (`KLR.KLGamma.prop_3_20`),
the string parametrization of simples along any colour sequence meeting every colour
infinitely often (KL I §3.2, as used before Webster arXiv:1309.3796v2 Def. 5.16: the
lexicographically largest string vector in the character determines the simple; defined with the
highest-weight spaces `HWSpace`, which KL Lemma 3.13 identifies with `ẽ`;
`KLR.KLRAlgebra.isStrMax_aux`, `KLR.strKey_injective`), and
Cor. 3.19 as `End(S) = 𝕜` for every simple `R(ν)`-module (`KLR.KLRAlgebra.exists_eq_smul_of_isSimpleModule`),
so the bases of `K_0` and `G_0` are exactly dual (`KLR.GradingDatum.pairing_k0Basis_g0Basis_eq`).

**Twisted bialgebra structure.** `K_0(R(ν) ⊗ R(ν')) ≅ K_0(R(ν)) ⊗ K_0(R(ν'))`
(`KLR.GradingDatum.k0TensorEquiv`, from `S_b ⊠ S_{b'}` simple and `P_b ⊠ P_{b'}` indecomposable), so
`[Res]` is a coproduct `Δ` on `K_0(R)`; `Δ` is multiplicative for the twisted multiplication (3.1)
(KL I Prop. 3.2, `KLR.KLGamma.coprod_mul`) and coassociative (`KLR.KLGamma.coassoc`), and `γ`
intertwines Lusztig's `r` with `Δ` (`KLR.KLGamma.coprod_gammaZ`, `KLR.KLGamma.map_gammaF_rbar`). All
of this is bundled in `KLR.KLGamma.theorem_1_1_bialgebra`: `γ` is an isomorphism of `ℕ[I]`-graded
twisted bialgebras (with Lusztig's `r̄` on `f` compared after base change to `ℚ(v)`).


### KLR algebras (Khovanov–Lauda I, arXiv:0803.4121v2; Khovanov–Lauda II, arXiv:0804.2080v1)

The ring `R(ν)` is defined by generators (idempotents `e i`, dots `x a`, crossings `ψ j`) and
relations, for an arbitrary family of polynomials `Q i j ∈ k[u, v]` (the generality of KL II).
The simply-laced rings of KL I, for a simple graph `Γ`, are `KLR.R1 k Γ ν` (`KLR.klQ Γ`) with
`Q_ij = u + v` for adjacent `i, j` and `Q_ij = 1` otherwise. Positions are zero-indexed.

The basis theorem (the paper works over `ℤ`) is proved over every commutative ring, for every
matrix `Q` with `Q_{ji}(u,v) = Q_{ij}(v,u)` and no condition on a Cartan datum or on leading
coefficients (`KLR.KLRAlgebra.basisOfSymm`); this is the generality of R. Rouquier,
arXiv:0812.5023v1, Thm. 3.7, which also shows the symmetry condition is necessary. Spanning holds
over any commutative ring (`KLR.KLRAlgebra.span_eq_top'`). Over an integral domain, linear
independence is proved by expanding the action on the polynomial representation over the fraction
field in terms of the automorphisms `w ∈ S_m` (`Categorification.PermExpansion`), rather than by
the paper's induction on sequences (`KLR.KLRAlgebra.basis`); the general case follows by base change
from universal symmetric data over a domain.

The detour through a universal domain is needed: over a ring with zero divisors the polynomial
representation need not be faithful. Rouquier, arXiv:0812.5023v1, Prop. 3.12, asserts faithfulness
for every commutative ring and every factorization `Q_{ij}(u,v) = P_{ij}(u,v) P_{ji}(v,u)`. Over
`ℤ/4` with `Q_{ij} = Q_{ji} = 2`, every such factorization has one factor equal to `2` up to a unit,
so one of `2τ1_{(ij)}`, `2τ1_{(ji)}` acts by zero although it is nonzero (it is twice a basis
element); over a field, `Q_{ij} = 0` gives a similar example. Faithfulness holds exactly when every
`P_{ij}` (`i ≠ j`) is a non-zero-divisor in `k[u,v]`, the hypothesis Rouquier adds in
arXiv:1112.3619v1, §3.2. Thm. 3.7 itself holds over every commutative ring, as above. (This
observation is not formalized.)

### Iterated flag varieties (Khovanov–Lauda III, §4–5, first part)

The cohomology rings of partial flag varieties in their presentation by the elementary symmetric
functions of the blocks with total Chern class `1` (KL III (5.2)–(5.3), `Flag.H`), identified with
the Borel model `k[x]^{S_{d_0}×⋯×S_{d_{n-1}}}/(Sym⁺)` (`Flag.hEquiv`), of dimension the multinomial
coefficient (`Flag.finrank_H`); the fundamental theorem for Young subgroups (`Flag.youngEquiv`);
the maps between flag varieties of §5.1–5.2 with (5.7), (5.15)–(5.16), (5.25), (5.28)–(5.29),
Prop. 5.3, Lemma 5.4 (i), (iii), and the first map of Cor. 5.5. The `sl_n` root datum, weights of
compositions (5.18), and the 2-category `U→` of Definition 4.1 with the signed KLR polynomials
(4.10)–(4.14) (`KL3.Diagram.Signed.USigned`).

Towards the 2-representation `Γ_N` (§5–6): the extension of flag cohomology rings is free with
basis powers of `ξ` (`Flag.eBasisRight`, `Flag.eBasisLeft`) and the Frobenius traces; Lemma 5.4
(ii), (iv) and both maps of Cor. 5.5; the caps (6.4)–(6.5) of Definition 6.1 as bimodule maps and
all four zigzag identities (`Flag.snake_E_FE`, …); dotted bubbles as Chern classes, the bubble
relations (3.4) and the Grassmannian relation for real bubbles (`Flag.grassmannian`); the
divided-difference crossings (6.8) with the nilHecke relations, and distant crossings. On the
word model of Def. 5.6 / (6.1) (iterated tensor products of the step bimodules,
`Flag.gammaP`): all upward crossings (same, distant and adjacent colours, the latter via a
push-forward from the non-flag product), their dot slides and double crossings at every position,
matching the signed relation (4.11) of `U→(sl_n)` (`KL3.Diagram.Signed.gammaCross_sq`); the
cups and caps of Def. 6.1 with all four zigzags on arbitrary 1-morphisms (`Flag.zigzag_E`, …),
cyclicity of dots (`Flag.cycDot_F`) and dotted bubbles (`Flag.bubbleFEW_eq`); the braid
relations (4.13) and (4.14) with the signed polynomials on three strands of any word whose
intermediate regions are compositions (`Flag.braid_three`, `KL3.Diagram.Signed.braidQ_signed`);
the downward crossings (6.9) (`Flag.crossDn_eval`); the curl relations with KL's indices and the
fake bubbles (`KL3.Diagram.Signed.curlRW_eq_curlRHS`, `Flag.curlLW_eq`); the sideways crossings
of distinct colours (Lemma 6.4) and cyclicity of distinct-colour crossings. For adjacent colours
the latter two differ from the arXiv v1 text by a sign: the sideways crossing
`F_j E_i → E_i F_j` is minus the swap when `i = j + 1` (`Flag.crossrW_one`), so the relations
`eq_downup_ij-gen` hold only up to sign (`Flag.downupEF_W`, `Flag.downupFE_W`), and the two
rotations of an adjacent-colour crossing differ by `-1` (`Flag.rotCrossRW_eq_neg_rotCrossLW`).
Khovanov–Lauda's *Erratum to "A categorification of quantum sl(n)"*, Quantum Topol. 2
(2011), 97–99, successfully corrects Lemma 6.4: for distinct colours its two maps agree
with `Flag.crosslW_one` and `Flag.crossrW_one` and their dot-slide extensions. Its revised
Definition 4.1 is formalized literally (`KL3.Diagram.Signed.presSignedQT`, homogeneous,
differing from the v1 definition `KL3.Diagram.Signed.presSigned` only in the adjacent-colour
relations). The two-rotation cyclicity equation on p. 97 also holds on `Γ_N`
(`Flag.rotCrossRW_add_rotCrossLW`). The residual discrepancy is in the mixed relations
(3.13) on p. 98 and the same signs in the Proposition 6.6 amendment on p. 99: retaining
the specified flag maps and upward KLR normalization requires `j - i` in the first
display and `i - j` in the second, opposite to the printed scalars. Equivalently, fixing
upward colour `i` and downward colour `j`, both products of the sideways maps act by
`j - i` for adjacent colours (`Flag.downupEF_W_consistent`, `Flag.downupFE_W_consistent`).
The disagreement requires admissible flag steps and an element not killed by `2`
(`Flag.downupEF_W_ne_literal`, `Flag.downupFE_W_ne_literal`), not merely adjacency.
The corrected presentation is `KL3.Diagram.Signed.presSignedQT'`; these are also the mixed
signs of Cautis–Lauda (arXiv:1111.1431v3, (2.16), Prop. 6.3) for `t_{ij} = i - j`.

There is a related downward-normalization issue when the Proposition 6.5 amendment is
compared with **arXiv:0807.3250v1**, (6.9) and (6.16), pp. 74, 77. Writing `A, D, B`
for the three maps in (6.16), from left to right, the amendment gives `A = D = -B`
for adjacent colours, whereas the specified maps give `A = -D = -B`
(`Flag.rotCrossRW_eq_crossDn`, `Flag.rotCrossLW_eq_crossDn`). Thus the amended relation
between the outer rotations is correct; matching the middle downward crossing requires
a consistent normalization. This comparison is with arXiv v1, not a separate collation
of the 2010 journal printing. The two-rotation relation alone does not fix the bare
downward generator. These local sign/normalization issues do not disprove the principal
categorification theorem or rule out an abstract category defined by the literal relations.

Cyclicity for equal colours (`Flag.rotCrossRW_same_eq`,
`Flag.rotCrossLW_same_eq`) and the `EF`/`FE` decompositions of Prop. 6.3
(`KL3.Diagram.Signed.decompEF_W`, `decompFE_W`, with the degenerate regions) hold on the word
model, as do the `R(ν)`-relations in the degenerate regions where an intermediate 1-morphism is
zero (`KL3.Diagram.Signed.braid_cases`, `braidL_degenerate`, `braidR_degenerate`,
`degen_cross_sq`). So every relation of the revised Definition 4.1 (with the corrected (3.13)
signs) is verified on `Γ_N`. For the assembly as a 2-functor: the target (path bimodules on
realized compositions, zero objects otherwise; `Flag.gammaFunctor`), the interchange law, and a
lifting theorem reducing the construction of `Γ_N : P ⥤ ModuleCat` for any presentation `P` on
the `sl_n` signature to the vanishing of each relation on the word model (`Flag.gamma_respects`,
`Flag.gammaLift`). Every relation of Cautis–Lauda's `U_Q(sl_n)` with `t_{ij} = i - j` holds on the
word model (`Flag.gammaN_hP`), so `Γ_N` is a 2-functor `U_Q(sl_n) → ModuleCat` (`Flag.GammaN`) and,
composed with `Σ`, out of KL III's `U(sl_n)` (`Flag.GammaN_KL3`); with CL's normalization the
downward crossing of adjacent colours `j, i` goes to `t_{ji}⁻¹` times KL's (6.9). Lemma 5.4 (iii) as printed
in (5.45), with `x(k)_{i+1,·}`, is false as we read it (a counterexample with `N = 2`:
`Flag.Erratum545.lemma_5_4_iii_printed_false`); the version with `x(k)_{i,·}`, which is the one
used in the proof and in Cor. 5.5, holds (`Flag.lemma_5_4_iii`).

**Nondegeneracy ([#71](https://github.com/apellis/categorification-lean/issues/71)).**
`GenericFields.calculusNondeg_charZero` constructs the rational input from the
proved generic evaluation and the explicit diagonal symmetrizer. The integral
interfaces `integralNondegBasis` and `calculusNondeg_of_rational` then give the
unconditional endpoints `canonicalBasis` and `calculusNondeg`. Direct unfurling
is used only in characteristic zero; bad-characteristic fields and rings with
zero divisors are covered by integral spanning and presentation base change.
`scripts/IntegralDescentCheck.lean` exercises the root import and the presented-Hom basis map
over `ZMod 4`, rather than assuming that specialization preserves linear independence.

The scalar products over the constructed generic fields now have an algebraically independent
polynomial action for arbitrary colour sets and finitely many label slots per colour:
`GenericFields.bubbleEval_indep` (Webster arXiv:1603.06311v3, proof of `nondegenerate`).
The hypotheses require enough negative-alphabet slots of level one; other levels are arbitrary.
`GenericFields.vacuumBubbleEval_indep` discharges the level hypotheses for the explicit
strand/bubble/balancing weights, leaving only `N ≤ b` for the number of bubble slots.
`bubbleEval_X_of_nonneg` and `bubbleEval_X_of_neg` express its generators in the two vacuum
`lwSeq` formulas, with the orientation and dot shift explicit. The categorical
vacuum edge products now reindex to these slots. `GenericFields.klVacuumBubbleMap`
is the polynomial map defined by evaluation of the generic KL family at its vacuum,
and `klVacuumBubbleMap_eq` identifies this actual map with `vacuumBubbleEval`.
`klVacuumBubbleMap_independent` proves bounded algebraic independence for `N ≤ b`,
and `klVacuumBubbleMap_scalar` gives the categorical scalar action on `klVacuum`.
These statements require `0 < b`, positive symmetrizers nonzero in the ground field,
and their Cartan compatibility; no scalar bubble equation is an input. The local
generator comparison and transport of scalar evaluations are proved before
specializing the generic family. `GenericFields.unfurledEval` constructs the
required evaluations with no scalar-law premise. The direct characteristic-zero
result and integral descent then prove nondegeneracy in every characteristic.

The transcription of Def. 3.1 is cross-checked by homogeneity of every relation and by cyclicity
of the generators with respect to the biadjunctions. The family of generic
representations now proves independence of the KL spanning diagrams.
In KL III (3.23) the second sum should run over `g_1 + ⋯ + g_4 = −⟨i,λ⟩ − 2` (zero when
`⟨i,λ⟩ > −2`), not `⟨i,λ⟩ − 2` as printed: this is forced by the grading and agrees with the
corresponding relation in Lauda, arXiv:0803.3652v3. The definition `E_𝐢1_λ := (E_{𝐢̂}1_λ, e_𝐢)` for
divided-power sequences (after (3.55)) should carry the grading shifts of (3.54); we use them
throughout (without them the isomorphisms of Prop. 3.24 are not of degree 0).

### Categorified quantum groups for general scalars (Cautis–Lauda, arXiv:1111.1431v3)

The revised `Σ` cup/cap formulas (erratum p. 99) are correct for all integral weights:
with source indices `i = 1, …, n-1` and `d_i = (-1)^i`, a cap has factor `d_i` when
its exterior weight satisfies `λ_i ≡ 0 mod 4`, and a cup when `λ_i ≡ 2 mod 4`;
otherwise the factor is `1`, in either orientation
(`CL.Sln.sigmaDatum_chi_cap`, `CL.Sln.sigmaDatum_chi_cup`, in `KL3.Diagram`).
The cup's exterior region differs from the region in the internal datum by a simple root.
These successful corrections do not repair the literal target's mixed signs. For example,
over `ℚ` in `sl₃` with `N = 2`, the flag sequence `(0,0,2,2)` admits both orders of
`E₁` and `F₂`. Their sideways composites are fixed by the prescribed `Σ` scalars,
but their product is the identity in the source and minus the identity in the literal
amended target. The tensor of units is not killed by `2`: compatible cohomology
augmentations send it to `1` in `ℚ`. Thus the specified isomorphism to that literal
target fails, while `sigmaEquivPres` proves the equivalence to CL's normalized target.
This is the same mixed-sign defect as (3.13)/Proposition 6.6, not an independent
cup/cap error or a separate failure of the principal theorem. The weight-dependent
downward factor is forced by CL's convention, not an additional printed `Σ` rule.

In CL §2.1.1, "`s_{ij}^{pq} = 0` when … `d_{ij} ≥ p` or `d_{ji} ≥ q`" should read "`p ≥ d_{ij}` or
`q ≥ d_{ji}`". In the last paragraph of the proof of CL Lemma 5.4, the value `β_0 = -r_i^2` should
read `β_0 = -r_i^{-2}`, the value in the statement of the lemma; it follows from
`c_0^+ = -1/(β_0 r_i)` and `c_0^+ = r_i` in the same proof (`StrongSl2.beta_eq_neg_one_zero`
proves `β_0 = -1` for dots normalized to `r_i = 1`). The downward KLR relations for the dual scalars `Q'` follow from `Q`-cyclicity,
as CL state, by rotating the upward relations (a rotation calculus that needs only the zigzag
relations, not cyclicity): adding them gives an isomorphic presentation
(`KL3.Diagram.CL.lin_relationDown`, `KL3.Diagram.CL.presCLDownEquiv`). The main theorem (CL
Theorem 1.1) is proved on hom categories for every (symmetrizable) Cartan datum (`TwoRep.QStrong.interpUQ`, see the end of this paragraph). Its proof rests on Prop. 3.9 (biadjointness is automatic),
whose adjoint induction has a gap at the step of Lemma 3.6: the final paragraph (decomposable
`E1_{n-2}`) uses Lemma 3.1 at weight `n-2`, which is not available at that stage, and the `n ≤ 0`
half (Remark 3.11) is vacuous at weight `0`. The induction therefore has no foothold at the weights
`{0, -2}` (even highest weight) or `{1, -1, -3}` (odd), although it propagates from any foothold
and closes unconditionally for highest weight at most `5`. The library proves Prop. 3.9 through the
condition `(BB_w)` (`StrongSl2.adjHyp`, below; issue #6). In `(BB_w)`, each pair of parallel words in `E`, `F`
has its own lower bound on graded Hom finranks. With Hom-finiteness this is actual Hom-space
vanishing. This is not a uniform bound or a condition on arbitrary ambient 1-morphisms.
**`(BB_w)` is a theorem** (`StrongSl2.bbw`): it follows from the formal adjunction
`E 1_n ⊣ 1_n F ⟨n+1⟩`, the isomorphisms (3), integrability, finite-dimensional 2-morphism spaces and
`Hom(1_n, 1_n⟨l⟩) = 0` for `l < 0`. The defect of the numerical adjunction between two
word-generated 1-morphisms is a two-sided sequence killed by a product of quantum integers (an
`sl₂` lemma over `ℚ[q, q⁻¹]` with the quantum integers inverted); the quantum integers have
nonnegative coefficients, so the defect vanishes as soon as one of the two Hom-sequences vanishes in
degrees `≪ 0`; an induction from the lowest weight then bounds every word. Hence CL Lemma 3.6 and
Prop. 3.9 hold as printed (`StrongSl2.dotNondeg`, `StrongSl2.dotNondegNeg`,
`StrongSl2.adjHyp`; Prop. 3.9 with the shift-coherence mixin), and no statement of the library
carries `(BB_w)` as a hypothesis. For `g = sl₂` the interpretation of `U(sl₂)` on hom
categories is constructed (`StrongSl2.interpU`, below). For every Cartan datum, a `Q`-strong
2-representation whose KLR action uses CL's polynomials and scalars `S₀` gives a linear functor
from the hom categories of `U_{S₀}(g)` (`TwoRep.QStrong.interpUQ`). For `d_i = (α_i, α_i)/2 ≠ 1` the
restriction to an `α_i`-string is a strong 2-representation of `sl₂` on the bicategory with the
grading shift `⟨n⟩` replaced by `⟨d_i n⟩` (`TwoRep.Regrade`; CL's "rescaling degrees", §2.6.1 and
§5), and its `sl₂` model is compared with the model of the `Q`-strong 2-representation through
the comparison of graded-Hom bicategories sending degree `n` to degree `d_i n`
(`TwoRep.Regrade.comparison`). The
mixed relations come from J. Brundan's relation chase (arXiv:1501.00350v1, Lemma 5.2), which
without the inverse of the sideways crossing `σ` gives a one-sided inverse of `σ` at every weight,
together with CL Def. 1.2 (5) and Hom-finiteness; CL's Lemma 6.1 (whose printed proof is
incomplete, issue #19) is not used.

`TwoRep/Coop.lean` constructs the bidual `Coop B` of a bicategory (1- and 2-morphisms reversed;
the Hom categories are opposite categories with the inverted grading shift, so degrees are
preserved: `Coop.finrank_hom_shift`), transfers the graded structure and adjunctions
(`Coop.gradedBicategory`, `Coop.isLinear`, `Coop.adjunction`, `Coop.adjunctionUnop`), and
identifies shifted 2-morphisms of the bidual with those of `B` (`Coop.shOp`, reversing
composition and exchanging left and right whiskering). `TwoRep/CoopCoherence.lean` proves that
shift coherence passes to the bidual (`Coop.shiftCoherence`). `TwoRep/Duality.lean` builds the dual
strong 2-representation on the bidual (`StrongSl2.dual`).

`TwoRep/ShiftCoherence.lean` bundles the existing coherence assumptions and proves the
degree-zero, unitor and associator identities for shifted whiskering (`shWhiskerLeft_mk₀`,
`shWhiskerRight_mk₀`, `shWhiskerLeft_of_id`, `shWhiskerRight_of_id`,
`shWhiskerLeft_of_comp`, `shWhiskerRight_of_comp`, `shWhisker_assoc`).
`TwoRep/GradedHom.lean` constructs the finite-direct-sum graded-Hom category with faithful
additive (and linear) degree-zero inclusion, homogeneous components, and homogeneity of inverses; `GradedHomBicategory.lean` extends shifted whiskering to an
actual bicategory using these coherence hypotheses. `GradedHomAdjunction.lean` transports a
given adjunction and proves degree-preserving bijections (`shiftedHomEquiv`, `shiftedEndEquiv`)
and naturality under arbitrary-degree composition (`Θsh_comp`). This constructs neither new
adjoints for a strong 2-representation nor the low-weight footholds; Prop. 3.9 is proved below through the
graded-endomorphism boundedness condition `(BB_w)`, which is a theorem (`StrongSl2.adjHyp`).

`TwoRep/RightwardCrossing.lean` works in any bicategory with preadditive Hom categories and
additive whiskering. It defines the rightward crossing `σ` from a unit, an upward crossing and a
counit (Brundan, arXiv:1501.00350v1, (1.6)), and proves the pitchfork relations, the mixed braid
relation (Brundan (2.4) for one colour) and the one-zigzag lemma (`adjunctionOfRightTriangle`: one
zigzag identity and an endomorphism ring of `f` without nontrivial idempotents give `f ⊣ g`). At
the weights `0` and `-2` it carries out Brundan's argument for Theorem 4.3 (claims (4.4), (4.5))
without bubbles (`rightZigzag_zero`, `rightZigzag_neg_two`). `TwoRep/AdjointWeightZero.lean`
applies this in the graded-Hom bicategory of a strong 2-representation: `StrongSl2.grSigma`
(homogeneous of degree `-2`), and `StrongSl2.adjHyp_of_wt_eq_zero`,
`StrongSl2.adjHyp_of_wt_eq_neg_two`, which prove (3.2) at weight `0` (resp. `-2`) **assuming**
that `σ_0` and Rouquier's map `ρ_2 = (σ_2, ε, ε ∘ x)` (resp. `ρ_{-2} = (σ_{-2}, η, x ∘ η)`) are
invertible and that `End(E1_n)` is one-dimensional. `TwoRep/RouquierMaps.lean` discharges these
hypotheses from the numerical shadow of (3.2) at the weights `> 0` (`StrongSl2.NumAdj`, which holds
at every weight by `numAdj`): `σ_0` is invertible
(`isIso_sigma_of_wt_eq_zero`: `Hom(EF1_0, FE1_0)` is one-dimensional, contains the isomorphism of
condition (3), and `σ_0 ≠ 0` because the crossing is recovered from `σ` by a cup and a cap,
`RightwardCrossing.cross_eq_of_sigma`), and `ρ_2` is invertible (`isIsoToSum₃_rho_two`: in a
decomposition `EF1_2 ≅ FE1_2 ⊕ 1_2⟨1⟩ ⊕ 1_2⟨-1⟩` the matrix of `ρ_2` is lower triangular with
invertible diagonal, the last diagonal entry being the subdiagonal dot entry of CL Lemma 3.6;
`RightwardCrossing.isIsoToSum₃_of_triangular`). Hence, assuming shift coherence and
idempotent-complete Hom-finite Hom categories, **(3.2) holds at weight `0`**
(`StrongSl2.adjHyp_wt_zero`) and, applying this to the dual 2-representation on the
bidual (`TwoRep/Duality.lean`), **at weight `-2`** (`StrongSl2.adjHyp_wt_neg_two`).
`TwoRep/AdjointPositive.lean` carries out CL's induction step at the weights `n ≥ 1`
(`StrongSl2.adjHyp_of_wt_pos`, from (3.2) above `n` and its numerical shadow at `n`). The unit is
the left cup (inclusion of the top summand of `EF1_{n+2}`). The counit is CL's cap (3.6). The zigzag
is a nonzero multiple of the identity by CL Lemma 3.8 (`cor2`), the nilHecke relation for `τ x₁ⁿ`
(`tau_comp_powComp`), CL Lemma 3.1 and Corollary 3.7. Hence, under the same hypotheses, (3.2) holds
at every weight `≥ 0` (`adjHyp_of_nonneg`), at every weight `≤ -2` by duality
(`adjHyp_of_le_neg_two`), so at every weight except `-1` (`adjHyp_of_wt_ne_neg_one`), and
at every weight for strings of even weights (`adjHyp_of_even`). `TwoRep/AdjointWeightNegOne.lean`
proves (3.2) at the weight `-1` from (3.2) at the weights `< -1` (`adjHyp_of_wt_eq_neg_one`). The
zigzag of the normalized summand inclusion and projection of condition (3) at the weights `±1` is
`1` minus a composite through `F E E 1_{-3}`. That composite vanishes because, through the two
adjunctions of `E 1_{-3}`, it is a closed diagram on an endomorphism of `E E 1_{-3}` of degree `-4`
(CL Corollary 3.2 for `n ≤ 0`). Hence **CL Proposition 3.9 holds at every weight**
(`StrongSl2.adjHyp`), assuming shift coherence and Hom-finite, idempotent-complete Hom categories.

`TwoRep/RouqSl2.lean` starts Brundan's theorem (arXiv:1501.00350v1) **without grading
hypotheses**: in any bicategory with preadditive Hom categories, `RouqLoc` is Rouquier's data at
one weight `h` (right adjunctions `E ⊣ F`, dots, a crossing with the dot slides, and explicit
inverses of Rouquier's maps (1.8)/(1.9)) and `RouqSl2` the data along a weight string. From the
inverses it defines the leftward crossing, the `♠`-cups and `♣`-caps, the leftward cup and cap
(Brundan (1.14)–(1.18)) and the dotted bubbles of all integer degrees ((3.8), (3.9)), and proves
Brundan Lemmas 2.1 and 3.1. `TwoRep/RouqDual.lean` realizes Brundan's Chevalley involution
(Theorem 2.3) as `RouqLoc.dual`, data at the weight `-h` in the bidual, with the dictionary
exchanging cups and caps, `♠` and `♣`, and clockwise and counter-clockwise bubbles ((3.4),
(3.10)). `TwoRep/RouqGrass.lean` proves **Brundan Theorem 3.2, the infinite Grassmannian
relation** (`RouqLoc.infGrass`), with no condition on `End(1_λ)`.

The mixed upward source data retain Brundan's entire rectangular sum of deformation
coefficients. This is not automatically `CL.qCL`, whose sum is filtered by weighted degree.
`TwoRep.brundanQ_eq_qCL` and `BrundanUpKLR.toCL` give the precise specialization when the
excluded source coefficients vanish. This restricts coefficients, not the target bicategory.
The normalized source dots do not establish arbitrary-`r` soundness merely because `genImg`
accepts `Sc`; scalar rescaling is separate. Normalized `presNM` now descends through the
original horizontal ideal, with admissible interchange, linear component functors and a
pseudofunctor proved without zero objects. With the additional, separately named source
`BrundanMixedInversion` and the same explicit coefficient compatibility, `RouqFullCL`
proves full `presCL` soundness and quotient descent. This does not derive inversion from
the weaker `presNM` data: concrete cyclotomic or unfurling actions must prove the actual
mixed mates invertible. `RouqUnfilteredSoundness` and `RouqUnfilteredDescent`
remove the coefficient compatibility by using `presUnfiltered`: the same generator
images satisfy every relation and descend to a quotient pseudofunctor, including
both distinct-colour cyclicity and down-up relations. The #187 cyclotomic endpoint
is `KLR.CyclotomicAction.cyclotomicPresUnfiltered`, without `hhom`, using the proved
cyclotomic upward relations and mixed inversion. The old homogeneous action is retained.
Arbitrary-`r`
rescaling and redundancy of the explicitly added downward family remain separate.

`KLR/CyclotomicMixedInversionAffine.lean` and
`KLR/CyclotomicMixedInversionTensor.lean` prove the distinct-colour **affine**
rectangular crossing equivalence used in Kang–Kashiwara, arXiv:1102.4677v4,
Theorem 5.1. The source is the actual tensor
`R(γ+j)e(γ,j) ⊗_{R(γ)} e(γ,i)R(γ+i)`; the target is the full rectangular
corner, not an associated-graded quotient. Distinct colours kill the noncrossing
stratum. Coefficient absorption removes the redundant Mackey factors with both
inverses proved, and the equivalence and its inverse respect the two different
coefficient algebras. `tauEquiv` has the actual elementary formula with `τ = -ψ`.
The empty lower weight `γ = 0` is included. Injectivity assumes `KLRSymm Q` over
a commutative base ring; the tensor comparison needs only a commutative base.

`CyclotomicMixedInversionIdeal`, `CyclotomicMixedInversionQuotient`, and
`CyclotomicMixedInversionDescent` now prove the actual two-sided ideal image
equality and the **cyclotomic bimodule equivalence**. The source is
`RΛ(γ+j)e(γ,j) ⊗_{RΛ(γ)} e(γ,i)RΛ(γ+i)` and the target is the full corner
in `RΛ(γ+i+j)`, with explicit weight transport. Both inverse identities and both
outer coefficient actions are proved, with `τ = -ψ`; the empty lower weight and
actual absent-colour zero projectors are included. No ideal compatibility, kernel
exhaustion, flatness, or isomorphism is assumed. The equivalence assumes
`KLRSymm Q` over a commutative base ring; the ideal reduction alone
must not be mistaken for arbitrary-`Q` bijectivity. The original ordinary-adjunction
mixed mate is now identified and inverted on arbitrary modules at removable-colour
inputs `{i}+γ`, as recorded below. The extension to all weights, including blocks without the colour, is
`cyclotomicFamily_mixedInversion` (`BrundanMixedInversion` for the cyclotomic family), and the
cyclotomic Cautis–Lauda action is `cyclotomicPresCL`; both are listed below.

`TwoRep/ShiftedMates.lean` constructs mates from an explicit adjunction, preserving degree
while reversing composition (`mateSh_comp`), with identity, additive and injectivity laws.
Scalar compatibility uses explicit linear Hom/whiskering/shift hypotheses.
`TwoRep/ShiftedMatesWhisker.lean` proves compatibility with composite adjunctions and both
shifted whiskerings. Its `StrongSl2` consumers identify tensor-factor dots and crossings on
the supplied weight-shifted right adjoints, and transport square-zero and both two-strand
dot-slide relations. `TwoRep/ShiftedMatesReassociate.lean` proves mate compatibility with the
actual associator and transports the degree `-6` braid relation to the two actual local crossings
on one parenthesized triple of shifted right adjoints (`mateCross_braid`).
`TwoRep/ShiftedMatesUnshift.lean` removes object shifts by actual homogeneous isomorphisms,
giving `fDot` on `F` and `ffCross` on `FF`, preserving degrees and detecting zero. Square-zero
and both original-scalar dot-slide relations hold on unshifted `FF` with transported dots
`ffDotFirst` and `ffDotSecond`. The conjugating tensor isomorphism is proved
(`ShiftRemoval.removeIso_tensor`). `TwoRep/DownwardNilHecke.lean` proves shift removal
commutes with both tensor factors, identifying these dots with actual whiskerings of `fDot`.
The resulting two dot-slide relations retain the original scalar `rQ`. Actual local crossings
on the left-associated unshifted `FFF`, with the second using the actual associator, satisfy
the degree `-6` braid (`fffCross_braid`). No all-strand action or automatic biadjointness is
claimed; the supplied adjunctions and graded coherence hypotheses remain explicit.
`TwoRep/DownwardNilHeckeFarComm.lean` constructs actual left-associated nonempty downward
words, with dots and adjacent crossings indexed from the right and zero outside the word.
It proves dot commutativity, crossing/nonincident-dot commutativity, and disjoint-crossing
commutativity at arbitrary width. Its width-three crossings are the existing local braid
generators. `TwoRep/DownwardNilHeckeAction.lean` additionally proves square-zero for
every crossing at every width and position, including the associator-conjugated bottom
pair and out-of-range positions, and supplies the native endomorphism-ring relation.
This module does not yet construct an action. `TwoRep/DownwardNilHeckeNormalization.lean`
certifies the two-strand convention `D = -rQ⁻¹ C`, with right-counted dots and reversed
endomorphism-ring multiplication: both native mixed dot relations and square-zero hold
for the actual operators. The original scalar is already a unit, so no new nonvanishing
hypothesis is added. `TwoRep/DownwardNilHeckeSlides.lean` propagates both normalized
mixed relations to every valid position `i < n` on the actual `n + 1`-strand word,
including associator-conjugated bottom pairs and recursively whiskered upper positions.
The homogeneous and native endomorphism-ring equations use the same scalar normalization
and existing graded-linearity hypotheses. `TwoRep/DownwardNilHeckeBraid.lean` proves braid
at every valid position using genuine associators and right whiskering; uniform normalization
and zero extension give the native endomorphism-ring braid at every index.
`fWord_isNilHeckeFamily` assembles all eight relations for the actual dots and normalized
crossings on every nonempty downward word, including the one-strand boundary.
`TwoRep/DownwardNilHeckeRepresentation.lean` supplies the natural componentwise linear
structure on the existing finite-sum graded Hom categories and constructs the actual
algebra homomorphism `fWordNilHeckeHom` from `NH_(n+1)` into the endomorphism algebra
of `fWord n r`. Its generator evaluations are the existing right-counted dots and
`-rQ⁻¹`-normalized crossings, and these evaluations determine the map uniquely.
The separate `fEmptyNilHeckeHom` acts on the genuine identity 1-morphism, retaining
the distinction between zero factors and `fWord 0 r` (one factor). Faithfulness,
automatic biadjointness, and the unrestricted Cautis–Lauda theorem are not asserted.

`TwoRep/DownwardDividedPower.lean` constructs `nhF` from the actual downward dots
and crossing on `F (r + 1) ≫ F r`, retaining the original unit-valued scalar `rQ`.
It splits the degree-zero idempotent in the original Hom category to obtain
`exists_F2`, the decomposition `FF ≅ F^(2)⟨1⟩ ⊕ F^(2)⟨-1⟩`, and
`exists_F2_dot`, an actual first-dot isomorphism on the common summand.
Idempotent completeness is assumed only of the original Hom categories, not the
finite-sum graded-Hom category. Supplied adjunction and grading-coherence hypotheses
remain explicit. This supplies downward divided-power input for the negative-weight
route, not Lemma 3.6, automatic biadjointness, or Proposition 3.9 themselves.

`TwoRep/StepLemmasNeg.lean` supplies a negative-weight induction-step vanishing
lemma using the actual downward nilHecke operators. If `wt (r + 1) ≤ 0` and
`AdjHyp` holds strictly below `r`, every negative-degree endomorphism of `F (r + 1)`
vanishes after right whiskering by `F r`. A corresponding retract consequence
applies only after that composition. Neither vanishing before whiskering nor a
per-summand lower-Hom bound follows.

`TwoRep/DotMateBridge.lean` proves that the original upward `dotFE` slides through
the supplied, explicitly shift-corrected adjunction cup to its actual downward
`fDot`. After right whiskering by `F r` and reassociation, the downward operator is
exactly `nhF.xL`, the first-factor dot used by `exists_F2_dot`. This is a local
cup identity, not equality of endomorphisms on all of `FE` or on arbitrary summand
inclusions of a chosen `FEDecomp`.
`TwoRep/DecompositionCupTransportNeg.lean` now handles the lowest inclusion `ιN e 0`
of every actual negative decomposition. Under `wt (r+1) < 0`, nonzero weight identity,
Hom-finiteness and `∀ s < r+1, AdjHyp s`, the supplied adjunction and existing
`lem1Neg_zero` prove the cup Hom space is one-dimensional. Both cups are nonzero,
so after explicit source-shift removal the decomposition cup is a nonzero scalar
multiple of the supplied cup. The original `dotFE`/`fDot` slide therefore holds at
`ιN e 0`, both in the original Hom category and after `F r` whiskering/reassociation,
where the downward operator is exactly `nhF.xL`. This induction premise includes
`AdjHyp r`; it is not the stricter cutoff `s < r` used by `StepLemmasNeg`.
No `AdjHyp` is derived. Along this direct route, higher-inclusion transport and the negative
multiplicity/rank argument remain open; `DotNondegNeg` and automatic biadjointness
are proved through the duality and `(BB_w)` (a theorem, `StrongSl2.bbw`) instead
(`StrongSl2.dotNondegNeg`, `StrongSl2.adjHyp`).

### KL I §3.3–3.4: relations between induction functors, tight monomials

`𝓕_i^{(a)} M = Ind(P_{i^{(a)}} ⊠ M)`; for any relation `∑ u_k θ(k) = ∑ v_ℓ θ'(ℓ)` in `_A f` with
`u_k, v_ℓ ∈ ℕ[q, q⁻¹]` there are isomorphisms `⊕_k 𝓕_{θ(k)}^{⊕u_k} ≅ ⊕_ℓ 𝓕_{θ'(ℓ)}^{⊕v_ℓ}` on graded
projectives and on all graded modules (KL I Thm. 3.21: `KLR.KLGamma.theorem_3_21`,
`KLR.KLGamma.theorem_3_21_gmod`, `KLR.KLGamma.theorem_3_21_natIso`), and conversely. Prop. 3.22 without the
canonical basis: if `(θ, θ) ∈ 1 + qℤ[[q]]` then `END(P_θ)` is one-dimensional in degree `0`, vanishes in
negative degrees, and `P_θ` is indecomposable (`KLR.KLGamma.prop_3_22`); in particular `P_{i^{(m)}}` is
indecomposable (`KLR.KLGamma.prop_3_22_single`). (The criterion for tightness is printed as
`(θ, θ) − 1 ∈ qℕ[q]`; since e.g. `(θ_i, θ_i) = (1 − q²)⁻¹`, power series are meant.)

### Headline: Khovanov–Lauda II, Theorem 8 (isomorphism)

For an arbitrary symmetric Cartan datum and a field `𝕜`, `γ : _A f → K_0(R)` is a ring isomorphism,
`ℤ[q, q⁻¹]`-linear, sending divided-power monomials (divided powers in `v_i = v^{i·i/2}`) to the classes
of the corresponding projectives: `KLR.KL2Gamma.theorem_8`, `KLR.KL2Gamma.gammaInt2Equiv`,
`KLR.KL2Gamma.gammaInt2Equiv_dpowMono`; over `ℚ(v)`, `KLR.KL2Gamma.gammaF2Equiv`. Again the quantum
Gabber–Kac theorem enters as an explicit argument, discharged by
`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.quantumGabberKac` (`KLR.KL2Gamma.theorem_8_unconditional`). The coproduct `Δ = [Res]` is multiplicative for the
twisted multiplication and coassociative, and `γ` intertwines Lusztig's `r` with `Δ`, so `γ` is an
isomorphism of `ℕ[I]`-graded twisted bialgebras (`KLR.KL2Gamma.theorem_8_bialgebra`).

### Headline: Khovanov–Lauda III, Theorems 1.1–1.2 (global integral algebra)

The source is the actual integral modified quantum group `AUD RD vQ`; the target
`K0All RD k` is the direct sum of the Karoubi-envelope Grothendieck groups over all
weight pairs, with multiplication induced by composition.

| Theorem | Source locator | Declaration (`KL3.Diagram`) | Hypotheses | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Global non-unital algebra map, respecting weight idempotents and Laurent scalars | KL III §3.6, Prop. 3.27 | `gammaAlg`, `gammaAlg_one`, `gammaAlg_smul` | Field; `TorsionFreeK0` (supplied by `HomGdim`) | Proved on `AUD → K0All` |
| Global surjectivity | KL III Thm. 1.1 | `gammaAlg_surjective_unconditional` | Field; finite colour set; arbitrary Cartan/root datum | Proved, without `SimplyLaced` |
| Global injectivity and equivalence | KL III Thm. 1.2; Webster Thm. 3.6 | `gammaAlg_injective_unconditional`, `gammaAlgEquiv_unconditional` | Field; finite colour set; arbitrary Cartan/root datum | Proved without a nondegeneracy premise; Laurent-scalar compatibility is `gammaAlg_smul` |
| Type-A global equivalence | KL III Thm. 1.3, Prop. 1.4 | `gammaAlgEquiv_sl` | Field; `slRootDatum m` | Proved without a nondegeneracy assumption |

The conditional core also supports arbitrary colour sets under explicit `HomGdim`
and `SortedSpan`. Exact hypotheses and the scope of the `_unconditional` wrappers
are documented in `Diagrams/KL3/GammaAlgebra.lean`.

Theorem 1.3 (nondegeneracy for `sl_n` over any field) and hence Proposition 1.4 (`γ` is an
isomorphism for `sl_n` over any field) are proved: `KL3.Diagram.theorem_1_3`,
`KL3.Diagram.prop_1_4_sl`. Instead of KL III's equivariant `Γ^G_N`, the spanning family
`B_{+i,+j,λ}` is separated by `Γ_N` of `sl_{n+1}` composed with the 2-functor
`U(sl_n) → U(sl_{n+1})` (`KL3.Diagram.SlnEmbed.embedU`, `Flag.Indep.linearIndependent_vB_sln`);
the extra block is needed because the non-equivariant `Γ_N` of `sl_n` does not separate the
bubbles when `char k` divides `n`.

### KLR algebras for arbitrary Cartan data (Khovanov–Lauda II, arXiv:0804.2080v1)

For a symmetric Cartan datum (`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum`), `KLR.R2 k C ν` is `R(ν)` with
`Q_ij = u^{d_ij} + v^{d_ji}` (`i·j ≠ 0`), `d_ij = −2 i·j / i·i`, graded by `deg x = i·i`.

### Cyclotomic KLR algebras (Kang–Kashiwara, arXiv:1102.4677v4)

Nondegeneracy of `U` for every finite Cartan datum (issue #71) is proved by
`KL3.Diagram.calculusNondeg`; `canonicalBasis` extends the diagram basis to
every commutative ring. The constructions below supply the proof. For any commutative ring `k`,
any index type `I`, KLR data `Q` and polynomials `a : I → k[X]` (Kang–Kashiwara's `a_i^Λ`, possibly
deformed), `KLR.CycKLR k Q a ν` is `R^Λ(ν) = R(ν) / R(ν) a^Λ(x_1) R(ν)`.

### Weighted KLR algebras (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4)

In progress (issue #289). Objects are chambers of loadings: a configuration of strands, indexed by
colour and rank, on no wall `x_b - x_a = ϑ_e`; two loadings lie in the same chamber exactly when
they are equivalent (the paper's Definition `def:equivalent`).

| §2.4, Proposition 2.18 (algebra level, generation, module isomorphisms) | partial. On idempotents, juxtaposition of full chambers is associative, `(C₁ ∘ C₂) ∘ C₃ = C₁ ∘ (C₂ ∘ C₃)` along `(ν₁ + ν₂) + ν₃ = ν₁ + (ν₂ + ν₃)`, and the chamber of `0` is a two-sided unit; `C' ∘ C''` contains every generic loading whose `ν''`-strands lie to the right of its `ν'`-strands by more than `∑_e \|ϑ_e\|`. On algebras, the juxtaposition map `ι_{ν';ν''} : W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''} → W_{ν'+ν''}` is associative with unit `1 ∈ W_0`. Generation clause, with `R_ν` (undefined in v4) read as the unweighted KLR algebra, realized as a matrix corner indexed by the family of evenly spaced loadings `B_s` (Proposition 2.14, `spacedEquiv`); the literal reading `R_ν = W^ϑ_ν` is false in general (Kronecker quiver; Corollary 2.16 covers only forests): the iterated juxtaposition of the units of the `W_{α_{iₘ}}` is `e(B_s(i₁ ⋯ iₙ))`, and `Ind(W e(B_s(i)) ⊠ W e(B_s(j))) ≅ W e(B_s(ij))`, so `W_ν e(B_s(i))` is the iterated induction of the regular modules `W_{α_{iₘ}}`. On modules, `Ind(Ind(M ⊠ N) ⊠ P) ≅ Ind(M ⊠ Ind(N ⊠ P))`, `Ind(W_0 ⊠ M) ≅ M ≅ Ind(M ⊠ W_0)` and `(Res ⊗ id) ∘ Res ≅ (id ⊗ Res) ∘ Res`, as `k`-linear isomorphisms semilinear along the identifications `castW` of `W_{(ν₁+ν₂)+ν₃}` with `W_{ν₁+(ν₂+ν₃)}` and of `W_{0+ν}`, `W_{ν+0}` with `W_ν` (restriction: along the associator of `W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂} ⊗ W_{ν₃}`). | §2.4, proof of Theorem 2.19 (after KL I Prop. 2.18): the degree of the twist of the Mackey filtration | for every grading datum, the straight gallery from the juxtaposition `C' ∘ C''` to the chamber with the block `C''` far to the left of `C'` (in either direction, for any chamber segment) has degree `∑_{a ∈ ν', b ∈ ν''} pairDeg(label a, label b)`, where `pairDeg i j` collects the walls of the edges between `i` and `j` in both directions and the diagonal if `i = j`; `pairDeg` is symmetric. For Webster's standing grading `pairDeg i j = -(i · j)` by inspection of the degrees (`WeightedGraph.Standing.pairDeg_eq_neg_cartanDatum_dot`, when every edge has distinct head and tail and every recorded loop polynomial is absent), so the shift is `-(μ · μ')`. More generally the degree of the straight gallery from `C` to `E` is the sum of the degrees of the hyperplanes separating `C` and `E`. | §2.4, proof of Theorem 2.19 (after KL I Prop. 2.18): the shuffle-type filtration of a corner | over any commutative ring, for rank-ordered full chambers `C`, `D` (Webster's chambers) and dimension vectors `ν'`, `ν₁`: the shuffle type of a relabelling `g` counts, for each colour `i`, the strands of the second block (rank `≥ ν' i`) that `g` moves into the first block (rank `< ν₁ i`). The operator of the basis element `b(g, m)` of Theorem 2.8 in the polynomial representation is supported in the relabellings of shuffle type `≤` that of `g` (triangularity). `mackeyFilt D C S` is the span of the `b(g, m)` with shuffle type in `S`; it has the basis of these elements, increases with `S` and is all of `e_D W e_C` for `S` everything. For a lower set `S`, `z ∈ e_D W e_C` lies in it iff the operator of `z` from `C` to `D` is supported in shuffle types in `S`; hence it is stable under left multiplication by elements whose operators preserve the first block at the top and right multiplication by elements whose operators preserve the second block at the bottom. Elements of `ι_{ν₁;ν₂}(W ⊗ W)` and `ι_{ν';ν''}(W ⊗ W)` have such operators: their block operators are supported in the relabellings preserving the first block of strands (block support is closed under products, and holds for the generators of a chamber embedding), so the steps are sub-bimodules for these actions. For sets of shuffle types `S' ⊆ S`, the quotient of the step `S` by the step `S'` is free on the classes of the `b(g, m)` with shuffle type in `S` but not in `S'` (`S' = S \ {μ}` gives the strict step of type `μ`). Up to the filtration, `b(g, m)` does not depend on the chosen minimal gallery: for any reduced word from `C` to `g⁻¹ D` (a good word), the element `r_g · gallery · m` lies in the step of `g`'s shuffle type and differs from `b(g, m)` by a combination of basis elements of that step with strictly shorter galleries. When the strands of `ν'` lie to the left in `C` and those of `ν₁` in `D` (as for juxtapositions; a chamber of `ν' + ν''` separated in this way is the juxtaposition of its restrictions), the gallery can be chosen to pass through two intermediate chambers: it first rearranges the two blocks of `C` separately, then performs the shuffle (crossing only hyperplanes between the strands of `ν'` and the strands sent into the first block of `D`, in opposite order), then rearranges the preimages of the two blocks of `D` separately; so `b(g, m)` is `r_g · c · shuffle · a · y^m` modulo basis elements of that step with shorter galleries. The outer pieces lie in the image of the juxtaposition map of the gallery algebras: a gallery from `C' ∘ C''` along which no strand crosses between the blocks is `ι(a₁ ⊗ a₂)` for galleries `a₁`, `a₂` of the factors, so the bottom piece with its dots is `ι(y)` and the top piece is `ι(x) · r_g` (relabelling by `g` moved past `ι`). The shuffle element `ψ_g = r_g · (galList along a fixed minimal gallery from the bottom to the top shuffle chamber)` does not depend on that gallery: the two shuffle chambers differ only on hyperplanes joining a strand of `ν'` to a strand outside `ν'`, and if every hyperplane separating two chambers joins two different sets of a partition of the strands, a flip block has exactly two hyperplanes (three would lie on the pairs of three strands, two in the same set), so all minimal galleries are related by exact square relations and give the same element. Hence `b(g, m) ≡ ι_{ν₁;ν₂}(x) · ψ_g · ι_{ν';ν''}(y)` modulo basis elements of the same step with strictly shorter galleries. Here `ι` is the juxtaposition map `galleryTensorHom` of the whole gallery algebras, not its restriction `inductionMap` to Webster's algebras: the intermediate chambers `shuffleBot`, `shuffleTop` are in general not rank-ordered, so `x` and `y` are not in general in `W ⊗ W`. Sorting the two ends of `ψ_g` into rank order inside the blocks (a separated chamber is a juxtaposition, and relabellings inside the blocks are juxtapositions) gives the shuffle element `ψ^W_g = r_u · ψ_g · r_{v⁻¹}` between rank-ordered chambers, fixed by Webster's idempotents `e_{ν₁;ν₂}` and `e_{ν';ν''}`; compressing by them, `b(g, m) ≡ ι(x) · ψ^W_g · ι(y)` with `x ∈ W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂}`, `y ∈ W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}` and `ι = inductionMap`, modulo the same shorter basis elements. `ψ^W_g` is the relabelling `u g v⁻¹`, of the same shuffle type as `g`, after a minimal gallery, so it lies in the step of every lower set containing that type; hence `x ⊗ y ↦ [e_D ι(x) ψ^W_g ι(y) e_C]` is a `k`-linear map from `(W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂}) ⊗ (W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})` to the subquotient (step `S` modulo step `S'`), and by induction on the length of the chosen gallery the images of these maps, over the `g` of shuffle type in `S` but not in `S'`, span the subquotient. Formerly open, now settled by step (ii) at the end of this cell: (a) balancedness of the maps over `ι(W ⊗ W)` on the two sides (descent to the induced module `W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂} ⊗ (twisted W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}-module) ⊗ W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}`, i.e. the intertwining of `ψ^W_g` with the four-block algebra). Toward (a): along a good word the identity coefficient of the operator of a gallery is the product `galNum / galDen` of the identity coefficients of its steps (wall polynomial, loop factor `P(X_l, X_r)` or `1 / (X_l - X_r)`); and for a four-block chamber `B` for `X` and `Z` the only relabelling dominated by `shuffleTop Z B` that sends, at every colour, as many strands outside `X` into `Z` as the identity is the identity, so the gallery from `B` to `shuffleTop Z B` has the identity as its only term of top type, with coefficient `galNum / galDen`. For `Z = blockLeft ν' ν₁ μ` and `ρ` rank-ordering `shuffleTop Z B`, `ρ` sends exactly `Z` into the first block of `ν₁` and has shuffle type `μ`, every other relabelling `x` in the operator of the gallery has `ρ x` of type `< μ`; `galNum`, `galDen` are fixed by the relabellings preserving the four blocks and agree for any two four-block chambers for the same blocks. Hence balancedness modulo lower types: for `U` supported in the block-preserving relabellings, `opList B' w' ∘ U - U ∘ opList B w` is supported in the `x` with `ρ x` of type `< μ`; in the algebra, if `u ∈ e_{B'} A e_B` acts by such a `U` and `u'' ∈ e_{ρ T'} A e_{ρ T}` acts by `ρ U ρ⁻¹`, then `fourShuffle Z B' * u - u'' * fourShuffle Z B` lies in the step `{< μ}`. Intermediate steps toward (a): the four-block juxtaposition `ι₄` (showing that its elements satisfy these hypotheses, `ι₄(a⊗c⊗b⊗d)` acting by the `ρ`-conjugate of `ι₄(a⊗b⊗c⊗d)`) and the descent of `typeInductionMap` to the balanced tensor product; (b) one map per shuffle type, proved for `C` separated by the strands of `ν'`: `ψ^W_g` is the four-block shuffle element `fourShuffle Z B` (the relabelling into rank order after a minimal gallery from a rank-ordered four-block chamber `B` to `shuffleTop Z B`; it does not depend on these choices) with `B = rankShuffleBot g C` and `Z = blockLeft ν' ν₁ μ`, the strands of `ν'` of rank `< ν₁ - μ` and of `ν''` of rank `< ν' + μ`, determined by the shuffle type `μ` of `g` (in a rank-ordered chamber a set of strands separating each block is an initial segment of each colour). Hence `ψ^W_g = ψ_μ · e_B` for the sum `ψ_μ` of `fourShuffle Z B` over all four-block chambers `B`, and since `e_B = ι(z)` for an idempotent `z` of `W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}`, the map of `g` is the map `x ⊗ y ↦ e_D ι(x) ψ_μ ι(y) e_C` precomposed with left multiplication by `z`, so its range lies in the range of the map of `μ`. Each summand `fourShuffle Z B`, for `μ` the shuffle type of some relabelling, is itself `ψ^W_y` with bottom end `B` for a relabelling `y` of shuffle type `μ` (relabel a `g` of type `μ` inside the blocks of `ν'`; the block sort of the rank-ordered `B` is trivial), so it lies in the step of every lower set containing `μ`. Hence the map of `ψ_μ` is a map into the subquotient, and the subquotient is spanned by the images of these maps over the shuffle types `μ` of relabellings with `μ ∈ S`, `μ ∉ S'`. A single `Y` per type does not span in general (on paper, not formalized: unweighted KLR of type `A₂`, `ν = 2α_i + α_j`, `ν' = ν`, `ν'' = 0`, `ν₁ = α_i + α_j`, `ν₂ = α_i`, `C = D = (iji)`; for `Y` the strands `j` and the second `i` the image lies in `e(iji) W e(jii) W e(iji)`, which kills `Ind(L(i) ⊠ L(ij))`, whose character has no `jii` term, while `e(iji)` does not). Comparing the images for different four-block chambers is the balancedness of (a); (c) injectivity, via `mackeySubquotientBasis`, and the degree shift of the row above Assembled over the corners, the filtration of the whole algebra: `bimoduleFilt S` consists of the `x ∈ W_ν` whose corner pieces `e_D x e_C` (`D`, `C` rank-ordered) lie in `mackeyFilt D C S`; it increases with `S`, is `0` for `S = ∅` and `W_ν` for `S` everything, its corner pieces are exactly the corner steps, and for a lower set `S` it is stable under `x ↦ ι_{ν₁;ν₂}(u) x ι_{ν';ν''}(w)`, so its steps are sub-bimodules (Webster's step (i)). Webster's step (ii), per shuffle type and for arbitrary weights: `resIndFilt S` cuts this filtration down to `e_{ν₁;ν₂} W_ν e_{ν';ν''}`. Take `S` a lower set whose only type outside a lower set `S' ⊇ {μ' < μ}` is `μ`, and write `ν' = a + b`, `ν'' = c + d`, `ν₁ = a + c`, `ν₂ = b + d`, `μ = c`. Then the subquotient `S / S'` is isomorphic to the balanced tensor product `(W_{a+c} ⊗ W_{b+d}) e' ⊗_{W_a ⊗ W_b ⊗ W_c ⊗ W_d} e (W_{a+b} ⊗ W_{c+d})`. The isomorphism intertwines `t ⊗ y ↦ z_L t ⊗ y z_R` with `[x] ↦ [ι(z_L) x ι(z_R)]`, so it is an isomorphism of bimodules. In particular the corner maps are balanced, as Webster's proof asserts. Injectivity and surjectivity are proved corner by corner, by the corner isomorphisms of the rows above. Tensoring the filtration and identifying its graded projective class gives the Mackey identity in the Theorem 2.19 row below. | `WeightedKLR.IsSupported`, `WeightedKLR.IsSupported.comp`, | §2.4, Theorem 2.19 | Proved over a field under the standing and (†) hypotheses for graphs without zero-weight loops: edge loops of nonzero weight are allowed for the coproduct, Mackey formula and braided Hopf structure; the quantum-group map stays loop-free (scope note below). A0 finite-dimensionality (`fdA`, `fdB`, `fdC`) is formalized under `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops`, `Γ.Standing`, with no loop-freeness assumption. Its centrality input: `WeightedKLR.GalleryAlgebra.poly_mem_center` proves that colour-symmetric dot polynomials are central in the gallery algebra, directly from Definition 2.4, without standing or loop-freeness hypotheses. A is formalized over any field and every standing `Q` for graphs with `Γ.NoZeroLoops` and no zero-weight vertex loops (`Γ.loop i = none`); edge loops of nonzero weight are allowed, since every local chart has zero weights and hence no loops: `WeightedKLR.exists_eq_smul_of_isSimpleModule` proves scalar endomorphisms of finite-dimensional simple modules with nilpotent dots, and `GradingDatum.exists_eq_smul_of_isGradedSimple` supplies the graded assertion. The proof chooses a minimum-norm point among closed supported chambers, discards basis terms that move it, and identifies the remaining action with that of a zero-weight local graph; matrix amplification and corner restriction then apply the ordinary KLR scalar theorem. This replaces the proposed right-restriction induction, which fails when all single-colour right restrictions vanish. `GradingDatum.tensorK0Hyp` supplies all five fields at arbitrary weights, including repeated colours, for any grading with `HasGdim`; positive dot degrees follow from that existing assumption. `exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded` therefore has no `TensorK0Hyp` hypothesis for these graphs. Graphs with zero-weight vertex loops would need absolute irreducibility for KLR algebras with loops, which KL I Cor. 3.19 does not cover; for them the conditional theorem remains as `exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded_of_tensorK0Hyp`. Proved over any commutative ring `k`, for the ungraded Grothendieck groups `K_0` of finitely generated projective modules: induction `Ind(P ⊠ Q)` of finitely generated projective modules is finitely generated projective, the product `[P][Q] = [Ind(P ⊠ Q)]` is a well-defined biadditive map `K_0(W_{ν'}) × K_0(W_{ν''}) → K_0(W_{ν'+ν''})`, and it is associative with unit `[W_0]`, up to the identifications `castW` of `W_{(ν₁+ν₂)+ν₃}` with `W_{ν₁+(ν₂+ν₃)}` and of `W_{0+ν}`, `W_{ν+0}` with `W_ν`. Under the standing hypothesis (with `Γ.Dagger` and `Γ.NoZeroLoops`, as for the basis theorem), restriction `Res_{ν';ν''} L` of a finitely generated projective `W_{ν'+ν''}`-module is finitely generated projective over `W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}` (KL I Prop. 2.16: `W_{ν'+ν''} e_{ν';ν''}` is a finite direct sum of modules `f (W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})`, `f` idempotent, and the reflection anti-involution moves this to `e_{ν';ν''} W_{ν'+ν''}`), so the coproduct component `Δ_{ν';ν''}[L] = [Res L]` is a well-defined additive map `K_0(W_{ν'+ν''}) → K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})`. Under the same hypotheses `Δ` is coassociative on `K_0`: `(Δ_{ν₁;ν₂} ⊗ id) ∘ Δ_{ν₁+ν₂;ν₃} = (id ⊗ Δ_{ν₂;ν₃}) ∘ Δ_{ν₁;ν₂+ν₃}` as maps into `K_0(W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂} ⊗ W_{ν₃})`, up to the associator and `castW`; here `Δ ⊗ id` and `id ⊗ Δ` are restriction along `ι ⊗ id` and `id ⊗ ι`, which preserve finitely generated projectives because `Res (φ ⊗ id) (B ⊗ C) ≅ Res φ B ⊠ C`. Toward the Mackey identity (Webster's step (iii)): tensoring an exact sequence of bimodules with a projective module over the balancing algebra is exact (`BalancedTensor.mapLeftBimodule_exact`), a short exact sequence of modules with projective quotient gives `[P₂] = [P₁] + [P₃]` in `K_0`, and a finite filtration with finitely generated projective subquotients gives a finitely generated projective module whose class is the sum of the classes of the subquotients. Tensoring a filtered bimodule with a projective module (Webster's step (iii), generic form): for a chain `0 = F 0 ≤ ⋯ ≤ F n = M` of sub-bimodules of a `(B, A)`-bimodule `M` and surjective bimodule maps `F (i+1) → Y i` with kernel `F i`, and `N` a projective left `A`-module such that each `Y i ⊗_A N` is a finitely generated projective `B`-module, `M ⊗_A N` is finitely generated projective and `[M ⊗_A N] = ∑_i [Y i ⊗_A N]` in `K_0(B)`. Applied to the Mackey filtration (under the hypotheses of Theorem 2.8 for `ν`, with `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops`, `Γ.PSymm`): the corner `e_{ν₁;ν₂} W e_{ν';ν''}` is a `(W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂}, W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})`-bimodule (`ResIndBimodule`), the shuffle types that occur are enumerated `f 0, …, f (n-1)` compatibly with the dominance order, the steps `resIndFilt (f '' {< j})` form a chain of sub-bimodules from `0` to the whole corner, and each step maps onto the four-block piece `ResIndPiece L = (W_{a+c} ⊗ W_{b+d}) e' ⊗_{W_a ⊗ W_b ⊗ W_c ⊗ W_d} e (W_{a+b} ⊗ W_{c+d})` of its shuffle type by a surjective bimodule map with kernel the previous step. Hence, for a projective `W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}`-module `N` such that each `ResIndPiece L ⊗ N` is finitely generated projective, `ResIndBimodule ⊗ N` is finitely generated projective and `[ResIndBimodule ⊗ N] = ∑ [ResIndPiece L ⊗ N]` over the shuffle types, one each (`exists_resInd_balancedTensor_eq_sum`). Under the standing hypothesis (with `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops`), `Res_{ν₁;ν₂} Ind X ≅ ResIndBimodule ⊗ X` as left `W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂}`-modules for every left `W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}`-module `X` (`resIndTensorEquiv`), and `ResIndPiece L ⊗ N` is finitely generated projective for every finitely generated projective `N` (`ResIndPiece.balancedTensor_projective`, `ResIndPiece.balancedTensor_finite`, through `e (W_{a+b} ⊗ W_{c+d}) ≅ Res W_{a+b} ⊠ Res W_{c+d}`). Hence the Mackey identity on `K_0` in piece form (ungraded, any commutative ring, standing hypothesis): for `ν₁ + ν₂ = ν' + ν''` there is one layout `L` per shuffle type such that `Δ_{ν₁;ν₂}(x · y) = ∑_L [ResIndPiece L ⊗ (x ⊠ y)]` for all `x ∈ K_0(W_{ν'})`, `y ∈ K_0(W_{ν''})`, with `W_{ν'+ν''}` identified with `W_{ν₁+ν₂}` by `castW` (`exists_resK0_indK0_eq_sum_tensorK0`; on classes `[P]`, `[Q]`: `exists_resK0_indK0_eq_sum`). No grading shift enters, since `K_0` is ungraded. Each piece is the twisted product of coproducts: writing `ν' = a + b`, `ν'' = c + d`, `ν₁ = a + c`, `ν₂ = b + d` for the layout, `ResIndPiece L ⊗ (P ⊠ Q) ≅ (W_{a+c} ⊗ W_{b+d}) e' ⊗_{(W_a ⊗ W_b) ⊗ (W_c ⊗ W_d)} (Res_{a,b} P ⊠ Res_{c,d} Q)`, semilinearly along `castW` (`ResIndPiece.resTwistEquiv`), so `[ResIndPiece L ⊗ (x ⊠ y)] = (Ind_{a;c} ⊗ Ind_{b;d}) ∘ (exchange) (Δ_{a;b} x ⊠ Δ_{c;d} y)` (`ResIndPiece.tensorK0_eq_twistK0`, with the external product `ProjK0.extTensor` and induction `Juxtapose.middleIndK0` along `(ι_{a,c} ⊗ ι_{b,d}) ∘ (exchange)`), and the Mackey identity reads `Δ_{ν₁;ν₂}(x · y) = ∑_L (Ind_{a;c} ⊗ Ind_{b;d}) ∘ (exchange) (Δ_{a;b} x ⊠ Δ_{c;d} y)` (`exists_resK0_indK0_eq_sum_twistK0`; same hypotheses). The right side lives in `K_0(W_{ν₁} ⊗ W_{ν₂})`, not in `K_0(W_{ν₁}) ⊗ K_0(W_{ν₂})`; the comparison needs a field (erratum below). Over any commutative ring, `Ind_{a;c}(P_a ⊠ Q_c) ⊠ Ind_{b;d}(P_b ⊠ Q_d)` is induction from the middle parabolic of `(P_a ⊠ P_b) ⊠ (Q_c ⊠ Q_d)` (`WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.extIndIndEquiv`; on `K_0`: `WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.middleIndK0_extTensor`), so the twisted product `T_L((x_a ⊗ x_b) ⊗ (y_c ⊗ y_d)) = (x_a · y_c) ⊗ (x_b · y_d)` on `K_0 ⊗_ℤ K_0` maps under the comparison `c : K_0(A) ⊗_ℤ K_0(B) → K_0(A ⊗ B)` to the twisted induction of the pieces (`WeightedKLR.ShuffleLayout.extTensorHom_twistedMulK0`). Under the explicit hypothesis that the ungraded comparison `c` is bijective for all pairs of dimension vectors (`ProjK0.ExtTensorBijective`, not proved for weighted KLR algebras; no available result gives it, and the graded `KLR.TensorK0Hyp` does not apply to ungraded modules), `Δ_{ν₁,ν₂} = c⁻¹ ∘ resK0` (`WeightedKLR.coproductK0`) satisfies the Mackey formula `Δ_{ν₁,ν₂}(x · y) = ∑_L T_L(Δ_{a,b} x ⊗ Δ_{c,d} y)` over the shuffle types, under the standing hypothesis and over any commutative ring (`WeightedKLR.exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum`, ungraded). Graded `K_0` (`Categorification/WeightedKLR/GradedK0/`): over any commutative ring and for every grading datum, `W_ν` is a graded algebra for its gallery grading (the corner at the rank idempotent, `GradingDatum.instGradedAlgebraKlrGrade`); over a field, under `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops` and the standing hypothesis, its graded pieces are finite-dimensional and vanish in sufficiently negative degree (`GalleryAlgebra.hasGdim_klrGrade`), so the graded `K_0` over `ℤ[q, q^{-1}]` is defined. Graded induction `Ind(P ⊠ Q) = W_{ν'+ν''} e_{ν';ν''} ⊗_{W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}} (P ⊠ Q)` of graded finitely generated projective modules is graded finitely generated projective, with no shift (`GradingDatum.indProj`), and gives a `ℤ[q, q^{-1}]`-bilinear product `K_0(W_{ν'}) × K_0(W_{ν''}) → K_0(W_{ν'+ν''})` (`GradingDatum.indK0`, `GradingDatum.indK0_of`) and the linear map `K_0(W_{ν'}) ⊗_{ℤ[q^±]} K_0(W_{ν''}) → K_0(W_{ν'+ν''})` (`GradingDatum.indK0Tensor`, `GradingDatum.indK0Tensor_of`). Over a field with both graded dimensions, `K_0(W_{ν'}) ⊗_{ℤ[q^±]} K_0(W_{ν''}) ≅ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})` with `[P] ⊗ [Q] ↦ [P ⊠ Q]` (`GradingDatum.k0TensorEquiv`, `GradingDatum.k0TensorEquiv_tmul`) under the explicit hypothesis `KLR.TensorK0Hyp` (graded simple tops of `W_{ν'}`, `W_{ν''}` and `W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''}` finite-dimensional, degree-zero endomorphisms of graded simple tops scalar); the source supplies none of these fields. A0 now supplies `fdA`, `fdB`, `fdC` under `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops`, `Γ.Standing`: every graded simple of `W_ν` or `W_ν ⊗ W_μ` is finite-dimensional (`GradingDatum.finiteDimensional_of_isGradedSimple`, `finiteDimensional_of_isGradedSimple_tensor`; for projective tops, `finiteDimensional_top`, `finiteDimensional_top_tensor`). This follows from finiteness over the central colour-symmetric dots and graded Nakayama (`CentralFinite.lean`, `Simple.lean`), adapting KL I Prop. 2.12. No loop-freeness assumption is used. A supplies `endA`, `endB` for graphs without zero-weight loops, with no restriction on colour multiplicities (`AbsoluteSimple.lean`, `TensorSimple.lean`). Graded pieces toward the Mackey formula: graded restriction on graded `K_0` (`GradingDatum.resGProj`, `resK0`, under `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops`, `Γ.Standing`), homogeneity of the steps of the Mackey filtration with graded steps and subquotients (`GalleryAlgebra.resIndFiltGrading`, `resIndSubquotientGrading`), the graded subquotient isomorphism of degree `middleShift` (`Juxtapose.middleResIndGradedEquiv`), and additivity of graded `K_0` on graded short exact sequences over a field (`Graded.K0.of_eq_add_of_exact`). Over a field, the graded chain step: a graded filtered bimodule tensored with a graded projective module has graded class the sum of the classes of its graded subquotients (`Graded.K0.exists_of_balancedTensor_eq_sum`); the pieces `ResIndPiece L` and the corner `ResIndBimodule` carry gradings (`ResIndPiece.grading`, shifted by `middleShift`; `GalleryAlgebra.resIndBimoduleGrading`), the subquotient isomorphism is graded for them (`Juxtapose.exists_middleResInd_gradedEquiv`), `resIndPieceMap` preserves the grading (`GalleryAlgebra.resIndPieceMap_preservesGrading`), and hence, under the hypotheses of Theorem 2.8 for `ν` with `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops`, `Γ.PSymm` and any grading datum, `[ResIndBimodule ⊗ N] = ∑_L [ResIndPiece L ⊗ N]` in graded `K_0` for graded projective `N` with finitely generated projective pieces (`GalleryAlgebra.exists_resInd_balancedTensor_eq_sum_graded`). Over a field, under `Γ.Dagger`, `Γ.NoZeroLoops` and `Γ.Standing`, the graded Mackey formula on graded `K_0` with the piece of each shuffle type shifted by `q^{middleShift}` (the degree `-(μ · μ')` of the shuffle diagram): `Res_{ν₁;ν₂}[Ind(P ⊠ P')] = ∑_i q^{s_i} [ResIndPiece (L i) ⊗ (P ⊠ P')]` (`exists_resK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded`, via the graded identification `resK0_map_indK0_eq` of `Res ∘ Ind` with a balanced tensor product with `Res Ind`). Each graded piece is the twisted induction of restrictions with no extra shift (the idempotent `e` has degree `0`): `[ResIndPiece L ⊗ (P ⊠ Q)] = (Ind_{a;c} ⊗ Ind_{b;d}) ∘ (exchange) (Δ_{a;b} [P] ⊠ Δ_{c;d} [Q])` in graded `K_0` (`ResIndPiece.of_tensorGProj_eq_twistK0Graded`, with graded induction from the middle parabolic `GradingDatum.middleIndK0`), so `Δ_{ν₁;ν₂}(x · y) = ∑_i q^{s_i} (Ind_{a;c} ⊗ Ind_{b;d}) ∘ (exchange) (Δ_{a;b} x ⊠ Δ_{c;d} y)` for all `x`, `y` (`exists_resK0_indK0_eq_sum_twistK0Graded`, same hypotheses). The graded analogue of `extIndIndEquiv` holds with no shift (`GradingDatum.middleIndGProjExtIndIso`; on graded `K_0`: `GradingDatum.middleIndK0_extTensor`, over a field), and for graphs without zero-weight loops `GradingDatum.tensorK0Hyp` proves the comparison for every pair of dimension vectors (the identification `K_0(W_μ ⊗ W_{μ'}) ≅ K_0(W_μ) ⊗ K_0(W_{μ'})` used without justification in the source), so the graded coproduct `Δ_{ν₁,ν₂} = k0TensorEquiv⁻¹ ∘ resK0` (`GradingDatum.coproductK0`) satisfies `Δ_{ν₁,ν₂}(x · y) = ∑_i q^{s_i} T_{L i}(Δ_{a,b} x ⊗ Δ_{c,d} y)` with the graded twisted product `T_L` (`ShuffleLayout.twistedMulK0Graded`; `exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded`, over a field, standing hypothesis). B and C are formalized: `KLR.Rouquier.K0_serre_window` proves the graded Serre relations over every commutative ring; `Standing.quantumGroupMap` maps the integral divided-power form into the weighted graded Grothendieck ring for loop-free graphs over any field under the standing and (†) hypotheses. `quantumGroupMap_generator` and `quantumGroupMap_dpow` identify the images, and `quantumGroupMap_smul` gives Laurent linearity with `q = v⁻¹`. The map has no additional comparison, Serre, scalar-injectivity or `HasGdim` hypothesis. `GradingDatum.K0W` is a Laurent algebra for every grading over any commutative coefficient ring. The total coproduct, associative twisted tensor multiplication and reduced antipode recursion are formalized (`GradingDatum.totalCoproduct`, `twMul`, `antipodeK0`). The counit laws, twisted multiplicativity (`GradingDatum.totalCoproduct_mul`) and right antipode identity hold. Graded triple restriction gives coassociativity (`GradingDatum.totalCoproduct_coassoc`). The weight braiding, counit, coassociative coproduct, and both antipode identities on the induction algebra are assembled in `WeightedGraph.Standing.braidedHopfK0`. Details and declarations are in `docs/WeightedKLR/thm-2-19-roadmap.md`. Open questions: (1) resolved: over a general commutative ring the theorem fails as printed (erratum below), so the coproduct statement is made over a field; for weighted KLR algebras over a field that is not algebraically closed the tensor identification also needs the graded simple modules to be absolutely irreducible, which is not proved in the source and is now formalized for loop-free graphs; (2) resolved: the degree of the shuffle diagram is given by the symmetric form `-(μ · μ')`, as in KL I; the printed `-⟨μ, μ'⟩` is correct with the pairing of §2.1, not with the §2.4 form `⟨j, i⟩` (row above, notation note below); (3) `𝔤_Γ` is not defined when `Γ` has loops; by the maintainer's decision the quantum-group map `Standing.quantumGroupMap` stays loop-free, while the coproduct and braided | §2.5, Proposition 2.20 | done. Reading (maintainer decision): the morphisms of the category `P` are the gallery algebra of the arrangement in positions and edge weights `(x, ϑ)`, with walls `x_b - x_a - ϑ_e` and diagonals (`TwChamber`, `TwGalleryAlgebra`, presented as the gallery algebra of one weighting with these chambers); when an interpolated weight of an edge `e : i → i` passes through `0`, a strand meeting its own ghost is no event, and the codimension-two flat `x_a = x_b`, `ϑ_e = 0` carries the diagonal-and-two-walls relations `{d, W, W.comap d.refl}` (Webster's (4a), (4b) for this edge). The source's definition (linear interpolation of `ϑ`, "the same local relations") is not closed under composition and does not cover this case. (a): the polynomial representation, one polynomial ring for each chamber, with the operators of Proposition `prop:action`; it restricts on each weighting to the representation of that weighting's gallery algebra (on steps, `twStepOp_toTw`). Hypothesis: symmetric loop polynomials (`PSymm`), as for `galleryRep`; no (†) and no hypothesis on edges from a vertex to itself. (b): for any choice `P` of simple segments between generic points of the chambers (the fixed stringing-up of each permutation), the elements `r g E * segGallery P C E * poly (y^m)` form a `k`-basis of `TwGalleryAlgebra k Γ ν`, over every commutative ring, by the proof of Theorem 2.8. Spanning needs no hypothesis on the graph: neither (†) nor the absence of loops of weight `0` (the hyperplanes of the arrangement are distinct, and every codimension-two flat, including the loop block, is the block of a braid move of the algebra). | arXiv:1309.3796v2, §4.7, Theorem 4.38, integral module and corrected Euler pairing | For any finite ordered red family, a finite simple-root index set, an X-regular root datum and Rouquier parameters over a field, the total graded projective K₀ is Laurent-semilinearly isomorphic to the integral tensor product. The vacuum summand is the Laurent integers. For the empty family it gives the tensor unit. With one red and one black strand the group is Laurent-free on the regular class for positive multiplicity and zero for multiplicity zero. The theorem is stated for graded right projectives, as in the source, and for graded left projectives. | arXiv:1309.3796v2, Proposition 3.25, Morita comparison | The Morita comparison of Prop. 3.25 with `End_𝒰(⊕ F_i λ)`, the input of Cor. 3.27 (maintainer decision 2026-11-21), is proved in the form below. Evaluation at `P_∅` is defined on the quotient by the ideal of 2-morphisms factoring through `F_𝐣 E_j 1_λ`, and this ideal contains the clockwise bubbles at the vacuum and the cyclotomic relation on the strand next to it (the right-hand side of the right curl relation); it is a two-sided ideal, and modulo it the bubble algebra at the vacuum is generated by the fake clockwise bubbles (by the infinite Grassmannian relation for general Cautis–Lauda scalars). Upward KLR diagrams at the region of `P_∅` give an algebra homomorphism from the KLR algebra, and evaluated through the cyclotomic Rouquier family they act as the image of the KLR algebra over `Ř^λ_λ`; their entries at the vacuum are the classes `hflip (σ (e(s) r e(t)))` in `Ř^λ(ν)`, and the vacuum entries of 2-morphisms in `I` vanish. The counterclockwise bubbles at the vacuum act on `P_∅` by the same traces, and bubbles to the right of upward strands evaluate, through any presentation whose relations hold, by right whiskering, so a bubble acting on `P_∅` by a scalar of `Ř^λ_λ` multiplies every vacuum entry by that scalar. The vacuum entries are multiplicative (the entry of a product is the matrix product of entries, in the reversed order), so the sum of all vacuum entries is a ring homomorphism to the opposite of `Ř^λ(ν)` that sends a KLR element `r` to `hflip (σ r)`. The fake clockwise bubble of colour `j` and degree index `n ≤ λ^j` acts on `P_∅` by `(-1)^n e_n(p_j)`, the Grassmannian inverse of the traces. Extending scalars, the KLR algebra over `Ř^λ_λ` maps to the matrix algebra of upward endomorphisms at the region of `P_∅`, with the scalars going to diagonals of fake clockwise bubbles, compatibly with evaluation at `P_∅`; the ideal `I` is stable under placing bubbles to the right of upward words. Every upward endomorphism at the region of `P_∅` is congruent modulo `I` to the image of this comparison map (upward spanning, from the spanning set of Prop. 3.10), and the universal cyclotomic polynomial in the dot next to the vacuum, with coefficients acting by fake bubbles, lies in `I` after placing it to the right of any word. Hence the cyclotomic ideal maps into `I`, the kernel of the vacuum-entry homomorphism consists exactly of the matrices with entries in `I`, and the homomorphism is surjective: modulo `I`, the upward 2-endomorphisms of the words of `ν` at the region of `P_∅` form the opposite of `Ř^λ(ν)`. More generally, for any two words at the region of `P_∅`, evaluation at `P_∅` has kernel exactly `I` and is surjective onto the morphisms between their images (the Morita step of Prop. 3.25). | `KLR.CyclotomicAction.exists_vacuum_up_retract`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.univVacuum`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.univVacuum_map₂_eq_zero_of_mem_vacIdeal`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.cwReal_mem_vacIdeal`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.curlRHS_mem_vacIdeal`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.mem_vacReduced_of_isBubC`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacGrassmannian`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacUpHom`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.upActP_vacUpHom`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacCoeff_vacUpHom`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacCoeff_eq_zero_of_mem_vacIdeal`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.univPres_ccwReal_vacuum`, `TwoRep.RouqFamily.upEvalP_bubAtC`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacCoeff_vacBubMat_mul`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacCoeff_vacBubMat_ccw_mul`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacCoeff_mul`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacRingHom`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacSum_vacUpHom`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.grassInv_vacTrace`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacEvalLin_cwL`, `KLR.KLRAlgebra.extendScalars`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacTheta`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacScalMat`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacSum_vacTheta`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.bubAtC_mem_vacIdeal`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.exists_vacThetaM_sub_mem`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacCurl_mem`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacThetaM_sigma_hflip_mem`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacRingHom_eq_zero_iff`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacRingHom_surjective`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.vacQuotientEquiv`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.mem_vacIdeal_of_vacEvW_eq_zero`, `KLR.CyclotomicAction.exists_vacEvQ_eq` |

| arXiv:1309.3796v2, Corollary 3.27 | Corollary 3.27 (1) (universality): for every 2-representation `𝒞` of the Cautis–Lauda presentation over `k` whose 2-morphism maps are `k`-linear, with finite biproducts and idempotent-complete weight categories, and every object `C₀` of the weight-`λ` category killed by every `E_i`, there is a strongly equivariant functor `Ř^λ-pmod → 𝒞` sending `P_∅` to `C₀`, unique up to a unique isomorphism fixing `C₀`; a strongly equivariant functor is determined up to isomorphism by its value on `P_∅`, and modifications by their component there.
