Errata to Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4 (Proposition 2.15 and the degree function of §2.1) and arXiv:1309.3796v2 ((2.2a–b), the pitchfork and downward relations (2.2a) and (2.5e), Proposition 3.31, §4.5, Lemma 4.35, and the form before Proposition 4.33 with Lemma 4.36) are in [ERRATA](ERRATA.md), with the printed and corrected statements, the declarations, and the open roadmap items.

The quotient and tensor product targets have a separate [roadmap](docs/WeightedKLR/tensor-product-roadmap.md).

Theorem 2.19, proof, antipode recursion. With the tensor operator `id ⊗ S`, the summand containing `S(x)` has first weight zero. The displayed exclusion of second weight zero retains this summand. Apply multiplication after `id ⊗ S` and exclude first weight zero. Equivalently, in positive weight start with `−x` and subtract the products over splits with both weights positive. On a single strand this gives `S(x) = −x`. See `GradingDatum.antipodeK0`.

Theorem 2.19, quantum-group map: the supporting polynomial conditions
`KLR.IsRouquierQ` and `WeightedGraph.Standing.isRouquierQ`, the Cartan datum
`Standing.cartanDatum`, and the leading-coefficient Serre identities
`KLR.Rouquier.serre_A`, `serre_B`, `serre_C` hold over arbitrary commutative coefficients.
Graded principal-projective induction (`GradingDatum.indK0_ofIdempotent`) and homogeneous
sequence-corner maps (`GradingDatum.spacedEntryMap_mem_klrGrade`) provide the weighted
transfer. The graded Serre relation is `KLR.Rouquier.K0_serre_window`, over every commutative
ring with Rouquier parameters and the canonical Cartan grading. The multiplicative
quantum-group map is `WeightedGraph.Standing.quantumGroupMap`, for loop-free graphs
over any field under the standing and (†) hypotheses; `rationalQuantumGroupMap`
is its scalar-extended counterpart. The map to integral `K_0` uses the integral divided-power form; the generic
`ℚ(v)` form requires scalar extension of the target. Proposition 2.14 is implemented as
a matrix loading-family corner, not necessarily a corner of the chamber algebra.
The generation clause of Proposition 2.18 concerns the ordinary KLR subcategory and does not imply surjectivity
onto weighted `K_0`; the middle-chamber example in the roadmap rules out that claim.

Erratum (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4, Theorem 2.19, ground ring). §2.1 fixes an arbitrary commutative ring `k`, and Theorem 2.19 asserts that `K = ⊕_ν K_0(W_ν)` is a twisted bialgebra over `ℤ[q, q^{-1}]` with coproduct given by restriction. Restriction lands in `⊕ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗_k W_{ν''})`, and the comparison map `K_0(W_{ν'}) ⊗_{ℤ[q^±]} K_0(W_{ν''}) → K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗_k W_{ν''})` need not be an isomorphism. Already for `ν' = ν'' = 0` (where `W_0 = k`) it is the multiplication map `K_0(k) ⊗_ℤ K_0(k) → K_0(k)` with `q^±` adjoined, which is bijective only if `K_0(k) = ℤ` or `k = 0`. For `k = ℤ × ℤ` with idempotent `e`, the class `[ke] ⊗ [k(1 - e)]` is nonzero and maps to `0`, for every `Γ`, `ϑ` and `Q`; the degree-zero part of `K` is then `K_0(k)[q^±]` rather than `ℤ[q^±]`, so the unit and counit are not those of a bialgebra over `ℤ[q^±]`. Corrected statement: for `k` a field (the convention of Khovanov–Lauda I, which the proof follows), with the coproduct composed with the inverse of the comparison map; for weighted KLR algebras this inverse exists when every graded simple module has endomorphism ring `k` (for example over an algebraically closed field). The parts that hold over any `k` (associativity and unit of the product, coassociativity and the Mackey identity of restriction into `⊕ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})`) are stated over any commutative ring. Formalized: the any-ring part of the product (associativity and unit of `[P][Q] = [Ind(P ⊠ Q)]` on ungraded `K_0`, `WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.indK0_assoc`, `indK0_one_left`, `indK0_one_right`) and coassociativity of restriction into `⊕ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})` (`WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.resK0_coassoc`, under the hypotheses of `res_projective`); the coproduct into `K_0(W_{ν₁}) ⊗ K_0(W_{ν₂})` and its Mackey formula are formalized ungraded under the explicit hypothesis that the comparison map is bijective (`WeightedKLR.coproductK0`, `WeightedKLR.exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum`, not proved), and over a field on graded `K_0` without a comparison hypothesis for loop-free graphs (`WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.tensorK0Hyp`, `WeightedKLR.exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded`); the conditional version for graphs with loops is `exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded_of_tensorK0Hyp`; A0 proves `fdA`, `fdB`, `fdC`, and A proves `endA`, `endB` for loop-free graphs over any field with standing `Q` (`WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.tensorK0Hyp`); the graded coproduct formula needs no comparison hypothesis in that scope (proof and scope in `docs/WeightedKLR/thm-2-19-roadmap.md`); roadmap row §2.4, Theorem 2.19 above.

Notation note (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4, proof of Theorem 2.19, grading shift). The proof states that the subquotients of the Mackey filtration of `Res ∘ Ind` are shifted by the inner product `-⟨μ, μ'⟩`. The shift is the degree of the diagram exchanging a block of strands of dimension vector `μ` with one of dimension vector `μ'`, and this degree is `-(μ · μ')` for the symmetric form `i · j = ⟨i, j⟩ + ⟨j, i⟩`, as in KL I Prop. 2.18: the exchange crosses each hyperplane joining the two blocks exactly once, and a strand of colour `i` and one of colour `j` contribute the walls of the edges between `i` and `j` in both directions (`WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.segDeg_chamber_swapChamber`, `WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.pairDeg_comm`). So the printed shift is correct when `⟨-, -⟩` is read as the symmetric pairing of roots of §2.1 (where `⟨α_i, α_i⟩` is the degree of a dot), and wrong for the non-symmetric form `⟨j, i⟩ = d_i δ_{ij} - ∑_{e : j → i} d_i c_e` defined just before in §2.4. For one edge `e : 0 → 1` with `d = c_e = 1`, exchanging one strand of each colour crosses one wall of degree `1` in both orders, while the §2.4 form gives `⟨α_1, α_0⟩ = 0`. The twisted product on `K_0` in the statement of Theorem 2.19 uses the symmetric form, so only the reading of the proof is affected.

**Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, Definition 7.1.** The word of the extremal projective
records the coroot pairing at each reflection. Along a path with nonnegative
pairings, its root content is the difference between the initial and final
weights (`Stendhal.rootSum_coevaluationWord`). For a reduced Weyl expression
from a dominant integral weight, the coroot pairings are nonnegative
(`Stendhal.nonnegativeReflectionPath_of_reduced`). For linearly independent simple
roots this determines the multiplicities
(`Stendhal.coevaluationWord_multiplicities_eq`). For reduced expressions of the
same Weyl element, this gives `Stendhal.coevaluationWord_multiplicities_eq_of_reduced`.
The terminal character of the two-red type A₁ algebra is homogeneous in the
Webster grading. Its derived restriction and extension give a graded
terminal-character cup and cap model over the rationals. The cup has internal
shift one and cohomological shift minus one
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.gradedFundamentalCup` and `gradedFundamentalCap`).

A graded diagram coefficient gives a dg bimodule of the two weight categories
(`diagramWeightBimodule`). Its derived tensor is computed on a K-projective
representative of the input (`diagramWeightDerivedTensorObjIso`). K-projectivity
of the left weight modules also gives the derived Hom
(`diagramWeightDerivedHom`). The Stendhal tensor product algebra depends only on the off-diagonal
polynomials. For a single black colour this identifies arbitrary parameter
choices (`tensorProductUnitParameterEquiv`). A K-projective augmentation that remains a quasi-isomorphism after
tensoring computes the derived tensor by the original ordinary tensor
(`diagramWeightDerivedTensorObjIsoOfResolution`). When the tensor target is isomorphic to a diagram
weight module, it suffices to check the cohomology map in degree zero and
vanishing in the other source degrees
(`diagramWeightLeft_isQuasiIso_of_cohomology`). The representable at a source weight is sent to
that component of the coefficient
(`diagramWeightDerivedTensorRepresentableIso`). The tensor functors on dg
modules, the homotopy category and the derived category are additive. At the
dg module level the tensor commutes with cohomological shifts naturally
(`diagramWeightTensorShiftNatIso`). The shift isomorphisms respect zero and
addition and descend to the homotopy category
(`diagramWeightTensorHomotopy_commShift`). Mapping cone comparison identifies
the standard triangles and makes the homotopy tensor triangulated
(`diagramWeightTensorHomotopy_isTriangulated`). The derived tensor is also
triangulated through the K-projective equivalence. Vanishing of its
cohomology at a target weight and degree passes through finite iterated
extensions (`diagramWeightDerivedTensor_cohomology_isZero_of_extensions`).

For Webster's Lemma 6.11, consider a cohomological degree of a K-projective
model generated by transporting loading-idempotent images along weight-category
arrows. If the idempotents kill the coefficient filtration layers, the derived
tensor cohomology vanishes in that degree
(`diagramWeightDerivedTensor_cohomology_isZero_of_filtered_loading_support`). For the auxiliary graded braid,
each loading defines an endomorphism at every source weight, and coefficient
annihilation is tested by left multiplication
(`auxiliaryGradedLoadingHom`, `auxiliaryGradedBraiding_cohomology_isZero_of_loading_support`).
The fundamental terminal standard realizes this criterion with the middle
projective generated at internal weight one. The terminal character kills
that loading, so its coefficient evaluation has vanishing cohomology in every
negative degree
(`gradedTerminalStandard_terminalCoefficient_cohomology_isZero`).

Finite filtrations by short exact sequences of dg weight modules localize
to iterated extensions of the localized layers
(`WeightModuleFiltration.extensionProductIter`). A homogeneous short exact sequence
restricts to a short exact sequence of weight modules
(`diagramWeightLeftLinearMap_shortExact`). For Lemma 6.11, vanishing of the
derived tensor on the localized layers gives vanishing on the filtered input
(`WeightModuleFiltration.diagramWeightDerivedTensor_cohomology_isZero`). A diagram of
ordinary complexes on the weight category realizes as a dg module, with
pointwise quasi-isomorphisms becoming dg quasi-isomorphisms
(`diagramWeightModuleOfComplex`, `diagramWeightModuleOfComplexMap_isQuasiIso`).
A homogeneous exact augmented chain gives a dg model with a pointwise
quasi-isomorphic augmentation (`weightDgGradedChainAugmentation_quasiIso`).
A bounded-above weight complex realizes as a K-projective dg module when
its individual terms, placed in their cohomological degrees, are K-projective
(`diagramWeightModuleOfComplex_isKProjective`). The ordered loading resolution meets this criterion because its
cohomological pieces are representable loading corners or direct sums of
such corners at their generator weights
(`orderedWeightDgResolution_isKProjective`). At a diagrammatic left loading standard, the graded
quotient and the higher trace filtrations supply the ordered K-projective
model and its quasi-isomorphic augmentation
(`leftStandardOrderedWeightResolution_isKProjective`,
`leftStandardOrderedWeightAugmentation_quasiIso`). At the standard Khovanov–Lauda parameters,
Corollary 5.21 supplies the higher trace filtrations directly
(`leftStandardOrderedWeightResolution_std_isKProjective`,
`leftStandardOrderedWeightAugmentation_std_quasiIso`).
The ordered loading resolution has a graded augmentation that is a
quasi-isomorphism to the homogeneous standard
(`orderedWeightDgAugmentation_quasiIso`).
The ordered model's higher loading support implies derived tensor concentration
when those loadings annihilate the coefficient filtration layers (Webster Lemma 6.11,
`orderedWeightDerivedTensor_cohomology_isZero_of_filtered_loading_support`).

A balanced homogeneous multiplication of two
coefficients induces their product from the intermediate weight-category
tensor coend (`diagramWeightCoendProduct_tmul`). Compatibility with the outer
action gives a map of dg modules (`diagramWeightCoendProductHom`).
Compatibility with both outer actions makes it natural in the source weight
(`diagramWeightCoendProductNatTrans`). Pointwise bijectivity gives the natural
isomorphism (`diagramWeightCoendProductNatIso`). In the right-module
convention this gives the coend multiplication of three rectangular corners
(`diagramCornerWeightRightCoendProduct_tmul`). If ordinary pure products
generate the coefficient, the coend product is onto at each pair of endpoint
weights (`diagramWeightCoendProduct_surjective`). The cohomology sequence at a fixed weight gives the
connecting isomorphism and kernel comparison
(`weightCohomologyShiftIso`, `weightCohomologyShiftKernelIso`). Forgetting the intermediate
weight embeds each endpoint coend as a summand of the ordinary additive
balanced tensor (`diagramWeightTensorSlice_leftInverse`). The associated
projection keeps pure tensors of the endpoint degree
(`diagramWeightOrdinaryProjection_tmul`). The image of the endpoint weight coend is exactly the total-degree
component of the ordinary balanced tensor (`diagramWeightTensorOrdinary_range_eq_grade`).
The integer and ground-ring balanced tensor gradings agree under the
additive comparison (`Graded.balancedTensorIntScalarAddEquiv_map_grade`).
The weight coend therefore identifies with the total-degree component
over the ground ring (`diagramWeightTensorGroundGradeEquiv`).
A nonpositive resolution has the same degree-zero tensor cohomology
as the ordinary weight tensor (`catTensorCohomologyZeroEquivOfQuasiIso`).
For a K-projective resolution, this identifies the cohomology of the
derived tensor at weight and degree zero
(`diagramWeightDerivedTensorH0IsoOfResolution`).
Evaluation at a weight and the K-projective model comparison are natural
in dg module maps (`localizedWeightCohomologyIso_naturality`,
`diagramWeightDerivedTensorObjIso_naturality`). For a rectangular corner between two degree-zero
idempotents, `diagramCornerWeightDerivedRightTensor` uses the restricted grading
and defines derived right tensor on the graded right-module categories.
For the actual braid bimodule this gives `auxiliaryGradedBraidingFunctor`
in the integer grading inherited from the augmented KLR algebra. For a two-red swap,
`twoRedSwapWeight` puts the actual rectangular coefficient in the complex
degree coset with offset `2 - ⟨λ_r, λ_d⟩`. The coordinate comparison
`twoRedSwapWeight_coordinate` and the reduced-diagram degree theorem
`twoRedSwap_mem_weight` identify those pieces with Webster's degrees.
The same comparison holds for any adjacent red swap with arbitrary spectator
strands (`adjacentRedSwap_mem_weight`). The normalized pieces decompose the
full coefficient as a direct sum (`adjacentRedSwapWeight_isInternal`).
For any red permutation, `redWordOffset` sums the crossing corrections over
the red pairs inverted between the boundary words. This defines the normalized
coefficient and its full homogeneous decomposition
(`redPermutation_mem_weight`, `redPermutationWeight_isInternal`).
The offsets add when each red pair crosses at most once. In that case the
actual multiplication adds normalized degrees
(`braidingCoefficientMul_mem_weight`). The inversion condition in Lemma 6.5
implies additivity (`redWordOffset_add_of_sublist`), and its balanced
multiplication identifies the quotient corner coefficient
(`violCornerTensorMulEquiv_tmul`). The weight-category product identifies
each endpoint coend with its homogeneous quotient coefficient
(`braidFactorizationCoendEquiv`). The equivalence respects the endpoint actions
as a natural isomorphism of dg module families
(`braidFactorizationCoendNatIso`). At a fixed source weight the product also
computes the derived composite whenever tensoring preserves the K-projective
augmentation (`braidFactorizationDerivedCoendOfResolution`). The source identity coefficient is representable at every
weight, so the corresponding derived composition requires no resolution
condition (`braidFactorizationDerivedCoendIdentity`). Its injectivity uses a split comparison to
the field corner tensor (`diagramCornerWeightRightSlice_leftInverse`).

Over a scalar field in degree zero the left modules are K-projective, so the
construction gives derived Hom for every homogeneous scalar coefficient
(`diagramWeightScalarDerivedHom`).
An ordinary bimodule map induces a natural transformation in the
coefficient of derived tensor. On a K-projective model this is the
ordinary tensor map applying it to the first factor
(`derivedTensorCoefficientMap`, `derivedTensorObjIso_coefficient_naturality`).
The degree-zero comparison with ordinary tensor is natural in this map
(`derivedTensorHomologyIsoOrdinaryLinear_coefficient_naturality`).
On a pure tensor it applies the map to the first factor
(`ambientH0CoefficientMap_tmul`). Naturality of the connecting morphism
transfers nonvanishing through a triangle when the source connecting
morphism is surjective (`homology_map_ne_zero_of_middle_next_isZero`).
It commutes with cochain shifts and defines a morphism between the
derived tensor images of a triangle (`derivedTensorCoefficientTriangleMap`).

For the ordinary corner tensor, `CornerTensor.liftAdd` descends a balanced
bi-additive family with an additive target. On rectangular coefficients it
sandwiches the inputs (`cornerTensorCutLift_tmul`).

Definition 7.4 has cup and cap coefficient models in the ungraded derived
categories, with a specified cohomological shift on the cup and a finite
dimensional ordinary tensor-Hom comparison preserving the module action
(`Stendhal.Coevaluation.shiftedCupFunctor`, `capFunctor`, and
`cupTensorHomEquiv_op_tmul`). The normalized graded coevaluation remains open.
For Definition 7.4, when twice the height or twice the inner-product
functional changes sign at the endpoint, the length and symmetrizer sum
of the coevaluation word compute the homological and internal shift numbers
(`coevaluationWord_length_eq_twice_height` and
`coevaluationWord_sum_symmetrizers_eq_twice_pairing`).

The cup also has an ungraded derived Hom construction with the action
by coefficient precomposition, with a dg Hom model on scalar inputs.
The cap using the same coefficient is left adjoint before the shift
(`Stendhal.Coevaluation.cupRHomObjIso` and `capBimoduleCupRHomAdjunction`).
The derived Hom and dual-tensor constructions agree on the ground field
(`Stendhal.Coevaluation.cupRHomTensorRegularIso`).
For the two fundamental type A₁ factors over the rationals, the dual
of the terminal character is the simple quotient by the smaller-cut trace.
It gives a terminal-character cup with homological shift minus one and cap
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.terminalQuotientDualEquiv`, `fundamentalCup`,
and `fundamentalCap`).
The cup on the ground field is the shifted terminal simple quotient,
and the cap on the regular module is the left coefficient
(`fundamentalCupRegularIso` and `fundamentalCapRegularIso`).
Webster's normalization in Proposition 7.5 uses the middle loading
`κ = (0, 1)`. The convention of §4 counts the black strands to the left
of each red strand. Over the rationals, the middle character gives the
simple head at this cut (`middleCutTrace_eq_iff_simpleQuotient`). Its
derived Hom cup sends the ground field to that head shifted by minus one
(`middleCup_fieldIso`). The unshifted left adjoint takes the middle
projective to the scalar line and kills the terminal projective
(`middleAdjointCap_middleIso`, `middleAdjointCap_terminal_isZero`).
The mixed crossings give a length-two projective resolution of the
middle head (`middleProjectiveResolution`). Its scalar Tor with the
left middle character has dimension one in homological degrees zero and
two and vanishes elsewhere (`middleTor_finrank`). With the cup shift,
the composite on the ground field has one-dimensional cohomology in
degrees minus one and one (`middleCupCap_finrank`). These computations
forget the internal grading.

For the fundamental labels in Proposition 7.11 (§7.2), the four loading corners
across the red crossing have dimensions zero (middle to middle), one
(each mixed corner), and two (terminal to terminal). The terminal basis
consists of the crossing and its one-dot decoration
(`fundamentalBraid_terminal_span`, `fundamentalBraid_terminal_linearIndependent`).
The mixed differentials on this basis have matrices `(0,1)ᵀ` and `(1,0)`
(`fundamentalBraid_terminal_differentials`). This computes the ungraded
corner diagrams entering the projective resolution. The surviving middle
class has diagram degree `1/2`. Its weight in the translated terminal
projective is exactly `3/2` (`fundamentalBraidMiddleClass_resolutionWeight`).

On the entire crossed loading cuts the first map is injective and the
second is surjective. The middle homology is the scalar line
(`fundamentalBraidCutComplex_homologyIso`). The weights admitting a homogeneous
representative of a nonzero class consist of three halves alone
(`fundamentalBraidHomology_weight_iff`). Both differentials preserve the
total weight. At three halves the restricted complex has a rational
homology line, and all other weight pieces are exact
(`fundamentalBraidWeightComplex_homologyIso`, `fundamentalBraidWeightComplex_exact`).
The graded diagonal-ring equivalence identifies the shifted loading projectives
in this resolution with the native projectives at the same weights in the
source of the braid (`fundamentalBraidSourceLoadingIso`). Its augmented
mixed-crossing sequence is exact at every source weight
(`fundamentalBraidSourceGradedResolution`). Tensoring these projectives with
the graded braid gives the crossed loading cut in auxiliary degree
`n-j` at target weight `n` for a generator of weight `j`
(`fundamentalBraidGradedCutIso`). On these coends both differentials multiply
by the mixed crossing (`fundamentalBraidGradedCutIso_d₂`,
`fundamentalBraidGradedCutIso_d₁`).
The resulting tensor complex has middle homology ℚ at auxiliary weight zero
(`fundamentalBraidGradedTensorHomologyIso`) and is exact at other weights
(`fundamentalBraidGradedTensorComplex_exact`). Its two ends are exact in every weight.
The iterated cone of the source loading projectives resolves the graded middle
head. Tensoring that cone represents the derived braid on this head
(`fundamentalBraidGradedDerivedModelIso`).
Its localized cohomology is ℚ in degree −1 and auxiliary weight zero
(`fundamentalBraidGradedDerivedHomologyIso`). It vanishes at every other
pair of degrees (`fundamentalBraidGradedDerivedCohomology_isZero`).
The action on that line is the middle character in the reversed red order.
In the derived category of target weight modules, the braid takes the graded
middle simple to the reversed middle simple in cohomological degree −1
(`fundamentalBraidGradedDerivedTargetIso`). Simultaneous transposition of
the red labels identifies the two positive crossing bimodules. Applying the
two successive positive crossings returns the ordered middle character
in cohomological degree −2 and auxiliary weight zero
(`fundamentalDoubleBraidHeadIso`). On the reversed cut the first crossing’s
physical offset of 3/2 makes the total offset 3
(`fundamentalDoubleBraidCut_physicalWeight`). The normalized cup contributes
internal translation one and cohomological shift minus one. Applying the
braid to this cup on the field gives the reversed middle character with
internal translation one, in cohomological degree zero
(`fundamentalBraidNormalizedCupIso`).
Graded red relabelling identifies the reversed-order cap with ordered evaluation
(`fundamentalBraidTargetCap_restrictIso`). Evaluating the braided cup in this
reversed order gives scalar lines of auxiliary weights −1 and 1 in
cohomological degrees 0 and −2, respectively (`fundamentalBraidCappedCupIso`).
For a scalar generator at weight j with shift [n], evaluating the braided cup
gives weights j−1 and j+1 with shifts [n] and [n+2]. This is the clockwise
circle on the same generator shifted by [1]
(`fundamentalBraidCappedCup_clockwiseIso`).
The diagonal-ring equivalence
carries the two mixed crossings to the differentials of the projective
resolution of the middle head (`fundamentalBraidSourceResolution`). Its
balanced tensor with the braid has this same homology line in resolution
degree one (`fundamentalBraidTensorCutIso`, `fundamentalBraidTensorHomologyIso`).
With the right action of the reversed-order ring, this homology is its
middle simple head (`fundamentalBraidRightTensorHomologyIso`). The positive
derived braid on the source middle head has this target head in native
cohomological degree −1 (`fundamentalBraidDerivedHeadIso`). Its cohomology
vanishes in every other degree (`fundamentalBraidDerivedHead_isZero`).

In the diagram-weight categories the middle character is homogeneous.
Its weight-zero generator identifies with the right simple head, with
every diagram acting by the middle character
(`gradedMiddleSimple_headEquiv_smul`). On the scalar representable the
normalized cup gives this character with internal shift one and
cohomological shift minus one (`gradedMiddleCup_fieldIso`). The
unshifted graded cap sends the middle loading projective to the scalar
representable of weight zero and annihilates the terminal loading
projective (`gradedMiddleCap_middleIso`, `gradedMiddleCap_terminal_isZero`).
Both differential crossings have diagram degree one. In internal degree
n the resolution is exact on the degree n−2, n−1 and n pieces of the
middle, terminal and middle projectives (`middleResolution_maps_degree`,
`middleResolution_exact_terminal`, `middleResolution_exact_middle`).
These maps and the scalar augmentation form an objectwise exact
sequence of dg modules over the weight category (`gradedMiddleComplex_exact`).
After applying the cap, the normalized composite on the scalar
representable splits into two scalar lines. Their internal and
cohomological degrees are (−1, 1) and (1, −1)
(`gradedMiddleCupCap_fieldIso`). For a scalar representable generated at
weight j with cohomological shift [n], the two output representables
have weights j−1 and j+1 and shifts [n−1] and [n+1], respectively
(`gradedMiddleCupCap_shiftedScalarIso`). For the clockwise orientation in
§8.1, the cap uses the derived tensor of Proposition 7.13. Its coefficient
has scalar fibers at equal weights, with right action given by the middle
character (`fundamentalTraceCoefficient_action`). The resulting fundamental
circle has compact scalar K₀ class −(q + q⁻¹) times the ground line
(`fundamentalClockwiseCircle_class`).

For Proposition 7.2, a single
red with coroot pairing one gives a simple projective at the red-first
one-black loading (`Stendhal.oneBlack_extremal_projective_simple`).
At highest weight two and two black strands, the image of the first dot
followed by the first crossing is an idempotent whose cyclotomic right ideal
is simple (`KLR.CycKLR.twoBlackTerminalProjective_simple`). The cyclotomic
equivalence gives the simple projective in the one-red Webster algebra
(`Stendhal.SingleRed.twoBlackTerminalWebsterProjective_simple`).
The rank-two saturated divided-power corner is the ground field
(`Stendhal.SingleRed.twoBlackTerminalWebsterCornerEquiv`). In §8.2,
Proposition 8.4, this is the common one-red factor of the two exterior
allocations. Pairing its actual right and left endpoint projectives by
balanced tensor gives this scalar corner. The left-derived pairing is the
ground field in degree zero and zero in positive degrees
(`Stendhal.SingleRed.labelTwoEndpointTorZero`,
`Stendhal.SingleRed.labelTwoEndpointTor_succ_isZero`).
The projector has diagram degree zero and the scalar corner has no
nonzero elements in other internal degrees
(`Stendhal.SingleRed.twoBlackTerminalWebsterIdem_mem_grade`,
`Stendhal.SingleRed.twoBlackTerminalWebsterCorner_mem_grade_iff`).
For both exterior allocations, projecting the actual parabolic
standardization with an affine lift of the separated projector cuts out
one scalar line. The projected action is independent of the lift
(`Stendhal.parabolicStandardConcatAction_eq_of_inputMap_eq`,
`Stendhal.SingleRed.labelTwoExteriorStandardCutEquiv`).
Sliding both blacks across the red intertwines their two nilHecke
projectors. The slide between complementary projectors vanishes
(`Stendhal.labelTwo_endpointSlide_projector`,
`Stendhal.labelTwo_endpointSlide_complement`). The complementary
terminal corner is spanned by the first dot and is concentrated in its
internal degree
(`KLR.CycKLR.twoBlackTerminal_reflected_corner_span`,
`KLR.CycKLR.twoBlackTerminal_reflected_corner_mem_grade_iff`).
The return crossing from the middle loading defines a map to the
complementary left-exterior divided projective. The two possible PBW
rows kill its image at the higher cut by the terminal square relation.
The map factors through the middle dot-sum quotient
(`Stendhal.labelTwo_leftExterior_return_square_zero`,
`Stendhal.labelTwoMiddleToExterior`, `Stendhal.labelTwoMiddleExteriorDifferential`).
The incoming exterior crossing descends to the middle dot-sum quotient
because its two PBW return words factor through the mixed braid
correction. The two maps on the divided-power terms form a complex
(`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorMiddleDifferential`,
`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorMiddleDifferential_comp_zero`).
The outgoing map on the two undotted terminal shuffles and their
first-dot translates has four explicit diagram columns. The undotted
second shuffle is zero, and its first-dot translate recovers the
undotted first shuffle
(`Stendhal.labelTwo_outgoing_terminal_affine_rows`,
`Stendhal.labelTwo_outgoing_terminal_diagram_columns`).
A polynomial flag representation of the actual two-red diagram
algebra detects the undotted and first-dot outgoing columns at both
surviving middle-standard loadings. Evaluating a relation on the
exterior unit vector recovers its two scalar coefficients
(`Stendhal.LabelTwoEndpointFlag.tensorFlagRep`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwoEndpointFlag.outgoing_middle_coefficients_eq_zero`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwoEndpointFlag.outgoing_terminal_coefficients_eq_zero`).
The projected exterior generator and its black-crossing translate
span the incoming divided-power standard. They map to independent
terminal coordinates in the common middle quotient, so the first map of
the actual short
complex is injective
(`Stendhal.labelTwo_rightExteriorDividedStandard_span`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoExteriorMiddleDifferential_injective`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoExteriorStandardComplex_mono_first`).
The middle quotient splits into its two loading cuts. The outgoing
map is injective at the middle loading. At the terminal loading its
kernel equals the span of the two incoming generator images. This
proves exactness at the middle term of the actual complex
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoExteriorStandardComplex_exact`).
The grading of the middle standard descends to its dot-sum quotient.
The complementary exterior projective inherits the regular algebra grading,
and the actual outgoing crossing has degree two between these graded modules
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoMiddleQuotientDecomposition`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoLeftExteriorDecomposition`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoOutgoing_degree_two`).
The divided incoming standard is graded using its degree-zero projected
generator. The actual incoming crossing also has degree two. The shifts
`3`, `1`, and `−1` on these raw gradings put both maps in degree zero
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoIncoming_degree_two`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoShiftedIncoming_preservesGrading`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoShiftedOutgoing_preservesGrading`).
The complementary exterior projective then surjects onto the actual
cokernel of the outgoing crossing
(`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorEndComplex_exact`,
`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorEndComplex_epi`).
The outgoing image contains the other loading cuts of the exterior
projective. Its cokernel is supported at the left exterior loading,
where the undotted generator and first dot span it. The black
crossing sends the first dot back to the nonzero generator, which
proves simplicity
(`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorAugmentation_loading_project`,
`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorCokernel_span`,
`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorCokernel_simple`).
The simple augmentation inherits the quotient grading from the exterior
projective. It preserves degree under their common shift by `−1` and
completes the exact sequence of graded diagrammatic terms
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoExteriorCokernelDecomposition`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoNormalizedAugmentation_exact`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoShiftedAugmentation_preservesGrading`).
The reflected complementary exterior projector selects the nonzero
first-dot line, in diagram degree two. The actual exterior projective
has ambient derived tensor homology only in degree zero for every right
coefficient (`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorReflectedLineEquiv`,
`Stendhal.labelTwoExteriorReflectedLineGenerator_degree_two`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoLeftExterior_derivedTensorHomology_isZero`).
The ambient derived tensor of the outgoing exterior term with its
horizontal reflection has degree-zero homology `k` and vanishes in every
other cohomological degree
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.outgoingExteriorAmbientHomology_zero`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.outgoingExteriorAmbientHomology_isZero`).
The reflected incoming exterior coefficient kills all strictly higher
loading idempotents. The ordered resolution and the divided projector
splitting show that the incoming diagonal ambient homology also vanishes
in every nonzero degree
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingExteriorAmbientHomology_isZero`).
The reflected incoming exterior paired with the middle standard vanishes
in every degree. The middle idempotent kills the ordinary tensor and the
higher loading support kills the remaining degrees
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingMiddleStandardHomology_isZero`).
On the incoming divided standard, the reflected projector selects the
line spanned by the black-crossing translate
(`Stendhal.labelTwoRightExterior_reflectedProjector_image`).
The translate survives by its terminal coordinate. Naturality of the
standard tensor fixed-cut equivalence identifies the ambient incoming
degree-zero diagonal with this scalar line
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingExteriorAmbientHomology_zero`).
Returning the lifted incoming generator to the unquotiented middle
standard gives its dot-sum relation. In the separated middle algebra this
relation survives modulo the second dot-sum image
(`Stendhal.labelTwo_incomingLift_return`,
`Webster.LabelTwo.plus_one_notMem_range_plus_comp_plus`).
At the middle loading, the preimage of the incoming image is exactly
the dot-sum image. The left-dot coefficient detects the first dot-sum
generator and vanishes on a second dot-sum action
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingLift_middle_fixed_iff`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleY₁Readout_diagramPlus_generator`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleY₁Readout_middleDotElt_incomingLift_zero`).
The middle left-dot coefficient descends through the dot-sum quotient in
the balanced tensor with the reflected incoming preimage and evaluates
the first dot sum on the middle generator to one
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingLiftTensorReadout_dotSum_generator`).
Over fields of characteristic different from two, this readout identifies the entire
balanced tensor with the ground field (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingLiftTensorEquiv`).
The incoming exterior map stays nonzero after tensoring with the reflected
preimage. The incoming translate tensored with the lifted generator
returns the first dot sum
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingLiftTensorMap_nonzero`).
The preimage, middle standard, and incoming cokernel form a short exact
sequence (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingLiftShortComplex_shortExact`).
The outgoing differential embeds the incoming cokernel in the exterior
projective with quotient the final simple
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.outgoingCokernelShortComplex_shortExact`).

The incoming divided exterior standard and the final augmentation
are simple. The middle quotient and outgoing projective each have a
proper nonzero submodule given by the image of the previous differential
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwoIncomingExterior_simple`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwo_middleQuotient_not_simple`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.labelTwo_outgoingExterior_not_simple`).







In any saturated one-colour content the coinvariant module gives a simple
module over the terminal cyclotomic quotient
(`KLR.CycKLR.terminalCoinvModule_simple`). At weight two the generators satisfy
the matrix-unit relations (`KLR.CycKLR.twoBlack_terminal_relations`).
For every multiplicity, the positive elementary symmetric polynomials in
the black dots vanish in the saturated cyclotomic quotient. The entire
coinvariant ideal therefore maps to zero
(`KLR.CycKLR.terminal_pol_esymm_eq_zero`,
`KLR.CycKLR.terminal_pol_eq_zero_of_mem_coinvIdeal`).
The actual saturated divided-power right ideal is simple for any number
of same-colour strands (`KLR.CycKLR.terminalDividedProjective_simple`).
Its one-red Webster image is a homogeneous simple projective, including
when the red weight has additional pairings at colours absent from the
black content (`Stendhal.SingleRed.saturatedWebsterProjective_simple`).
Identifying the divided-power projective at higher multiplicities and for
multi-letter words remains open.
Over a field, a projective with graded Hom norm one in a block of Laurent
rank one is simple as an ungraded module
(`GProj.isSimpleModule_of_homGdim_eq_one`). A nonzero degree-zero mixed Hom
identifies the graded lifts (`GProj.nonempty_iso_of_homGdim_coeff_zero`).
The classical and quantum weight spaces at the content of every reduced
coevaluation word have dimension one
(`Stendhal.finrank_quantum_coevaluationWeight`). For every content the
cyclotomic projective rank equals its quantum weight multiplicity. In
particular the cyclotomic coevaluation block has Laurent rank one
(`CycKLR.finrank_coevaluation_projectiveBlock`). At a positive Weyl step
the raised weight space vanishes in the classical and quantum modules, and
the full divided power preserves the Shapovalov norm on a bar-invariant
primitive vector (`SingleBlack.websterForm_saturated_dividedPower`). The
normalized divided-power cyclotomic projective of each reduced coevaluation
word is simple (`CycKLR.isSimple_coevaluationProjective`). Two reduced words
for the same Weyl element give isomorphic graded projectives
(`CycKLR.coevaluationProjective_iso_of_same_weyl`). The normalized
Shapovalov pairings are still needed for general reflection words.

**Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, §7.1, Lemma 7.3.** The smaller-cut trace is
the sum of the images of maps from the corresponding loading projectives
(`Stendhal.coevaluationTrace_eq_iSup_range`). A supported character and a
corner inverse test identify this trace with the unique maximal submodule
(`Stendhal.coevaluationTrace_eq_iff_simpleQuotient`). The mixed bigon factors
the terminal black dot power through the adjacent smaller cut
(`Stendhal.bigon_mem_coevaluationTrace`). For two fundamental type A₁
factors over `ℚ`, the terminal loading character has kernel exactly the
trace of the middle loading and gives the unique simple quotient
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.fundamentalCutTrace_eq_iff_simpleQuotient`).
The coefficient on the generating vector is one
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.terminalCoefficient_generator`). The general
diagrammatic inputs to the criterion remain open.
**Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, §8.2, Proposition 8.4 input.** The two black
dots on the actual middle standard of the label-two pair are commuting
square-zero endomorphisms. The polynomial quotient acts on this
standard, with the sum sent to the sum of the two right dots
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleDotQuotientAction`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleDotQuotientAction_sum`). PBW straightening gives
surjectivity and the two regular one-black factors detect four
independent coordinates. This identifies the actual endomorphism algebra
with the square-zero polynomial quotient
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleEndomorphismEquiv`).

**Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, §8.2, middle complex.** The alternating
dot maps give a complex of the diagrammatic middle standard, with a
right-module version by reflection and augmentation onto the dot-sum
quotient. At the middle loading both alternating pairs are exact and
the augmentation target is the dual-number specialization
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.rightMiddleComplex`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleCutDotSumEquiv`). At the terminal loading two independent
shuffle copies carry the same alternating dot maps. All other ordered
loadings act by zero. The alternating maps on the full diagrammatic
standard are exact and resolve the dot-sum quotient
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramMinusPlus_exact`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramPlusMinus_exact`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramMiddleAugmentation_exact`). The terminal augmentation
specializes each shuffle coordinate to a dual number
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.terminalCutDotSumEquiv_apply`). The incoming exterior diagram and
its black-crossing translate have coefficients `(1, −ε)` and `(0, 1)`
in these coordinates (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingDiagram_coordinates`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.incomingDiagram_translate_coordinates`).

The diagrammatic standard
is the parabolic standardization of its two regular separated factors
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardParabolicEquiv`). Its parabolic grading
pulls back to a graded module on the actual diagrammatic carrier
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardGradedEquiv`). Its middle generator
has degree zero, and both alternating dot differentials have internal
degree two (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramPlus_degree_two`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramMinus_degree_two`). The shifted differentials
and the augmentation to the graded dot-sum quotient are exact in every
internal degree (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramPlusMinus_exact_in_degree`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramMinusPlus_exact_in_degree`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramPlusAugmentation_exact_in_degree`).
The scalar and epsilon coordinates of the actual middle fixed dot-sum
quotient are in degrees zero and two
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleCutDotSum_mem_grade_iff`).
Tensoring these fixed vectors with the degree-zero standard generator gives
the graded ordinary middle tensor
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleFixedToOrdinaryTensorGradedEquiv`). Its ground-field
coordinates detect the internal degree
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardTensorEquiv_mem_grade_iff`). At this cut
the ordered cover transported from the source parameters has the literal
degree-preserving quotient augmentation
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleOrderedCoversTransport_quotient_mem_grade`).
The dot-difference quotient carries the companion grading. Both quotient
inclusions shift internal degree by two and give short exact sequences
in every degree (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramSumCompanionAugmentation_exact_in_degree`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.diagramDifferenceCompanionAugmentation_exact_in_degree`).
The inclusions and quotient maps give the corresponding distinguished
triangles of dg weight modules
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleSumWeight_distinguished`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleDifferenceWeight_distinguished`).
With vanishing standard cohomology, the connecting isomorphisms retain
the actual internal shift by two
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleSumWeightShiftIso`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleDifferenceWeightShiftIso`). The fixed-weight coend
of the reflected coefficient and middle standard agrees with the corresponding
ground-field component of the actual ordinary tensor
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeightTensorEquiv`). The degree-zero
derived comparison uses any supplied nonpositive K-projective model
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeightH0IsoOfResolution`). Internal translation of the input weights
induces the same translation of the output weights of the diagrammatic
derived tensor
(`diagramWeightDerivedTensorInternalShiftIso`, `weightInternalShift_cohomologyIso`).
For each companion quotient a grading shift by $j$ translates the output
weight from $w$ to $w-j$
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleSumWeightTensorCohomologyShiftIso`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleDifferenceWeightTensorCohomologyShiftIso`). The actual
graded middle standard extends to a higher-supported ordered resolution, and
the reflected coefficient has zero derived tensor cohomology in every
nonzero cohomological degree at each internal weight
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeight_cohomology_isZero`). In degree zero the
same ordered resolution gives one copy of the ground field at each of
internal weights zero and two, and vanishing at all other internal weights
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeightH0AtZeroIso`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeightH0AtTwoIso`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeightH0_isZero`). Evaluation on the

(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardWeight_cohomology_isZero`). The two companion triangles
alternate on the negative cohomological tail. Two steps raise the
cohomological index by two and decrease the evaluation weight by four
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleSumWeightTensorPeriodIso`). Evaluation on the
middle generator identifies the endomorphism algebra with the actual
regular parabolic factor (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleEndomorphismFactorEquiv`).
The diagrammatic dot-sum quotient is the standardization of its
dual-number factor specialization
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleDotSumStandardizationEquiv`).
The ideal of higher loadings is generated by the idempotent of
`r₀ b b r₁`, the unique nonviolated loading strictly above the middle
cut. The reflected dot-sum coefficient kills all higher loading
idempotents (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middle_upperLoadingIdeal_eq_singleton`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.rightMiddleDotSumQuotient_kills_higher`).
For any loading, quotienting the diagram algebra by the corresponding
higher-loading ideal makes the loading standard projective. Its generator
maps to the loading idempotent and splits off from the regular module
(`Stendhal.leftStandardLayer_projective`).
The actual periodic middle complex is consequently an augmented
projective resolution of the diagrammatic dot-sum quotient over the
middle layer algebra (`Stendhal.LabelTwo.relativeMiddleProjectiveResolution`).
A projective resolution of the actual middle standard whose positive
terms are supported on higher loadings gives ambient derived-tensor
vanishing outside degree zero for the reflected coefficient
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandard_derivedTensorHomology_isZero_of_higher_support`).
The standard rank-one parameter comparison supplies this supported
resolution for the actual middle cut
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.exists_middleStandard_supportedResolution`). The same
construction works for every ordered loading in the actual two-red,
two-black algebra
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.exists_loadingStandard_supportedResolution`).
The ordinary tensor of this actual coefficient with the middle standard
is the dual-number vector space at the middle cut
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardTensorEquiv`).
In these coordinates, the actual sum map vanishes and the actual
difference map is multiplication by `2ε`
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardTensorEquiv_plus`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.middleStandardTensorEquiv_minus`).
Tensoring the native projective resolution over the middle layer gives
the separated factor complex. Its relative Tor is dual numbers in
degree zero and one-dimensional in every positive degree over a field of
characteristic different from two
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.relativeMiddleTorHomologyIso`,
`Stendhal.LabelTwo.relativeMiddleTorPositive`).
The ambient tensor of the two actual sum quotients has the same Tor
objects degree by degree. This comparison uses the supported resolution
of the middle standard and dimension shifts from the two alternating
short exact sequences
(`Stendhal.LabelTwo.actualAmbientMiddleToRelativeTorIso`).

**Terminal-character resolution benchmark for Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2,
§8.1.** In the fundamental rank-one block over `ℚ`, the terminal simple
has a length-one projective resolution whose differential is the mixed
crossing from the middle loading to the terminal loading
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.fundamentalProjectiveResolution`). Its positive
self-Ext groups vanish
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.fundamentalTerminalSimple_ext_eq_zero`).

**Terminal-character cup and cap benchmark.** For two fundamental type A₁
factors over `ℚ`, the derived terminal-character cap sends the unshifted
terminal simple to the ground field in degree zero
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.fundamentalCap_terminalSimpleIso`).
Composing the terminal-character cup with this cap sends the ground field
to its shift by minus one
(`Stendhal.FundamentalCorner.fundamentalCupEvaluationRegularIso`).
Webster's v2 coevaluation cut `κ = (0, 1)` selects the middle loading in
Proposition 7.5.

### Weak `sl₂`-categorifications (Chuang–Rouquier, arXiv:math/0407205v2, §5)

Input for Webster's §5 (arXiv:1309.3796v2, Props. 5.11–5.18). A weak `sl₂`-categorification
(`Sl2.WeakSl2`, §5.1.1) is an abelian category with an adjoint pair `E ⊣ F ⊣ E` whose classes act
on a ℚ-vector space with the basis of simple classes as a locally finite `sl₂`-representation;
§5.1.1 also asks that every object have finite length (artinian and noetherian) and that
`End(S) = k` for simple `S`. NilHecke actions on `E^n` and `F^n` (§5.2.1) are `Sl2.NilHeckeData`,
with nilpotent dot.

### Lusztig's algebra `f` (Lusztig, *Introduction to quantum groups*, Ch. 1; KL I §3.1)

For a symmetric Cartan datum (`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum`, including the simply-laced datum of a
graph, `CartanDatum.ofGraph`) over a commutative ring with a unit `v`:

| Source | Result | Declarations |
|---|---|---|
| Lusztig 1.2.1–1.2.2 | the free algebra `'f`, the twisted algebra `'f ⊗ 'f`, and `r : 'f → 'f ⊗ 'f` | `QuantumGroup.PreF`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.TwSq`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.r`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.tensorEquiv` |
| Lusztig 1.2.3; KL I Prop. 3.3 | existence, uniqueness and symmetry of the bilinear form | `QuantumGroup.PreF.existsUnique_form`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.form_symm` |
| Lusztig 1.2.5 | `f = 'f / radical`, with nondegenerate form | `QuantumGroup.PreF.F`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.formF_nondegenerate` |
| Lusztig 1.4.3 | the quantum Serre relations hold in `f` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.serre`; simply-laced: `QuantumGroup.KL.serre_comm`, `QuantumGroup.KL.serre_cubic` |
| Lusztig 1.4.7 | divided powers and the integral form `_A f` | `QuantumGroup.PreF.dpow`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.Af` |
| Lusztig 1.4.1 | `θ_i^{(a)} θ_i^{(b)} = [a+b choose a]_i θ_i^{(a+b)}` | `QuantumGroup.PreF.dpow_mul_dpow`, `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.dpowF_mul_dpowF` |
| Lusztig 1.4.3 | divided-power form of the Serre relations; simply-laced: `θ_iθ_jθ_i = θ_i^{(2)}θ_j + θ_jθ_i^{(2)}` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.serre_divided`, `QuantumGroup.KL.serre_divided` |
| Lusztig 1.2.2, 1.4.2 | coassociativity of `r` on `'f`; `r` descends to `f`; `r(θ_i^{(a)})` | `QuantumGroup.PreF.coassoc`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.rbar`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.r_dpow` |
| Lusztig 1.4.4 | `(θ_i^{(a)}, θ_i^{(a)}) = ∏_{s=1}^a (1 − v_i^{−2s})^{−1}` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.form_dpow_dpow`, `QuantumGroup.KL.form_dpow_dpow` |
| Lusztig 1.2.12, 1.4.7 | the bar involution on `f`, preserving `_A f` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.barF`, `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.barF_mem_Af` |

The converse inclusion (the radical is generated by the Serre relations; Lusztig 33.1.3, used
in KL I §3) is `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.quantumGabberKac` (proved via lie-lean, see above). In the
list of generators of the ideal in KL I §3.1, `θ_iθ_j − θ_iθ_j` should read `θ_iθ_j − θ_jθ_i`.
In the right-module half of KL I Prop. 2.13, the summands should be the right projectives
`_{…i^{(2)}j…}P ⊕ _{…ji^{(2)}…}P`. In KL I §2.2, `L(i^m)` is nonzero in degrees `0, 2, …, m(m−1)`
(with `deg x = 2`), not `m(m−1)/2`.

### Graded modules and Grothendieck groups

Generic infrastructure over a `ℤ`-graded algebra (`Categorification.Graded`): graded modules via
Mathlib's `DirectSum.Decomposition` and `SetLike.GradedSMul`, grading shifts `M{a}` (shifted up
by `a`, as in KL I §2.5), graded dimension `gdim` in `ℤ((q))` (additive on short exact
sequences, `gdim (M{a}) = q^a gdim M`), the Grothendieck group `K0` of finitely generated graded
projective modules as a `ℤ[q,q⁻¹]`-module (with `[A e] = q^{-d}[A e']` for idempotents
equivalent via elements of degrees `d`, `-d`, and additivity on orthogonal idempotents), and
`G0` of finite-dimensional graded modules. For `R(ν)`: `P_i = R(ν) 1_i` and characters
`ch(M)` (`KLR.GradingDatum.projP`, `KLR.GradingDatum.ch`). "Graded projective" is taken to mean
projective as a module and graded. The forms on `K_0` are defined through `HOM` spaces:
`Graded.K0.homForm` (`([P], [Q]) = gdim HOM(P, Q)`, antilinear in the first variable) and
`Graded.K0.pform` (`(x, y) = homForm x̄ y` with the bar involution `[P] ↦ [P̄]`,
`P̄ = HOM(P, A)^ψ`; bilinear and symmetric). For `P` finitely generated graded projective and any
graded `N`, `m ⊗ n ↦ (f ↦ ψ(f(m)) n)` is a degree-preserving isomorphism
`P^ψ ⊗_A N ≅ HOM(P̄, N)` (`Graded.twistTensorGradedEquiv`, `Graded.gdim_twistTensor`), so the
paper's form `gdim(P^ψ ⊗_{R(ν)} Q)` is `pform` on all of `K_0`
(`Graded.K0.pform_of_eq_gdim_twistTensor`, `Graded.K0.eq_pform_of_gdim_twistTensor`), equivalently
`homForm x y = gdim(x̄^ψ ⊗ y)`, and the paper's pairing `gdim(P^ψ ⊗_{R(ν)} M)` with `G_0` is
`(x, y) ↦ pairing x̄ y` (`Graded.eq_pairing_bar_of_gdim_twistTensor`). On the self-dual classes
`[P_i]` all of these agree.

### The map `γ` (KL I §1, §3.1)

For the simply-laced datum of a graph (`KLR.KLGamma`): the classes `[P_{i_1^{(a_1)}⋯}]` in
`K_0(R)` with `[P_i][P_j] = [P_{ij}]`, `[P_î] = i![P_i]` and the Serre relations; the algebra map
`'f → K_0(R)`, `θ_i ↦ [P_i]` (over `ℤ[q,q⁻¹]`), which kills the Serre relations; its base change
`γ : f → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` (with `q ↦ v⁻¹`) sending divided-power monomials to the corresponding
`[P_{i_1^{(a_1)}⋯}]` (`KLR.KLGamma.gammaF`, `gammaF_dpowMono`), and the integral version on `_A f`
(`gammaInt`). Descending from `'f/(Serre)` to `f` uses the Gabber–Kac theorem, which enters as an
explicit argument `QuantumGroup.PreF.GabberKac` (discharged by `QuantumGroup.KL.gabberKac`); the integral map into `K_0(R)` uses that
`K_0(R) → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` is injective, which holds because `K_0(R)` is free (see below).

**KL I Prop. 3.4** (injectivity): Lusztig's form on words is
`(θ_{a_1}⋯θ_{a_m}, θ_{b_1}⋯θ_{b_m}) = ∏_x c_{a_x} · ∑_{w : b∘w = a} v^{∑_{(x,y) ∈ inv(w)} a_y·a_x}`
(`QuantumGroup.PreF.form_wordFn`), which matches `gdim(1_j R(ν) 1_i)` under `q ↦ v⁻¹`
(`KLR.KLGamma.lsCast_homForm_projP`); hence the kernel of `'f → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` is the radical of the
form, unconditionally (`KLR.KLGamma.ker_gammaQ_le_radical`), and `γ` is injective on `f` and on
`_A f` (`KLR.KLGamma.gammaF_injective`, `gammaA_injective`, `gammaInt_injective`, under the
hypotheses above). `γ` intertwines Lusztig's `r` with `[Res]` (`KLR.KLGamma.coprod_gammaZ`,
`KLR.KLGamma.map_gammaF_rbar`; see the twisted bialgebra paragraph at the top and
`KLR.KLGamma.theorem_1_1_bialgebra`).

### Modified quantum groups (Khovanov–Lauda III, arXiv:0807.3250v1, §2.1)

For a root datum over a field with `q` not a root of unity: Lusztig's `U̇` as the direct sum of its
blocks `U̇1_λ` (`QuantumGroup.UDot.UD`), its integral form, the involutions `ψ` and `ω`, the
bilinear pairing of KL III Prop. 2.2 (existence and uniqueness, `QuantumGroup.UDot.prop_2_2`,
`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_2`) and the semilinear form with the properties of Prop. 2.4
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_4`). Nondegeneracy of both forms (Prop. 2.5) for any Cartan datum,
under the Gabber–Kac inclusion of the radical of the form on `'f` into the Serre ideal
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5`); since `U̇` is defined by the Serre presentation, this
hypothesis is also necessary (`QuantumGroup.UDot.B_posF_radical`). It holds for every Cartan datum
(`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.quantumGabberKac`), so Prop. 2.5 is unconditional
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5_unconditional`; rank one directly:
`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5_sl2`). The proof
identifies the bilinear form with a tensor-product form on a model of `U̇1_λ` in `'f ⊗ 'f`
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.B_eq_ndP`), avoiding canonical bases. Theorem 2.7: the forms `(E_𝐢1_λ, E_𝐣1_μ) = ⟨E_𝐢1_λ, E_𝐣1_μ⟩` are the sum over
`(𝐢, 𝐣)`-pairings of `q` to the degree of a minimal diagram times the bubble contributions
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.thm_2_7`, any Cartan datum). In the example following Theorem 2.7, the
value printed as `1/(1-q_i)^2 · 1/(1-q_j)` should be `1/(1-q_i^2)^2 · 1/(1-q_j^2)`.

### Krull–Schmidt, projective covers, and the Grothendieck groups (KL I §2.5)

Over a field, for a `ℤ`-graded algebra with finite-dimensional graded pieces vanishing in
sufficiently negative degrees (`Categorification.Graded`): graded projectivity of projective graded
modules, Fitting's lemma, indecomposable graded projectives have local degree-0 endomorphism rings and
unique tops, every graded simple has a unique projective cover, `K_0` is a free `ℤ[q,q⁻¹]`-module
with basis the indecomposables up to shift (`Graded.K0.indecBasis`), `G_0` is free with basis the
graded simples (`Graded.G0.topBasis`), and the pairing `([P],[M]) = gdim HOM(P, M)` pairs the two
bases diagonally (`Graded.pairing_indecBasis_topBasis`). For `R(ν)`: `KLR.GradingDatum.k0Basis`,
`KLR.GradingDatum.g0Basis`, finitely many indecomposables (at most `(m!)²`), and consequently
`K_0(R) → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` is injective (`KLR.KLGamma.toK0Q_injective`), so the integral `γ` needs only
the Gabber–Kac hypothesis (`KLR.KLGamma.gammaInt'`). Krull–Schmidt also holds at the level of
modules: every graded f.g. projective is a finite sum of shifted indecomposables, uniquely up to
permutation, and `[P] = [P']` iff `P ≅ P'` (`Graded.GProj.exists_iso_sumList`,
`Graded.GProj.perm_of_iso_of_iso`, `Graded.K0.of_eq_of_iff`).

### Symmetric group combinatorics

Words in adjacent transpositions of `Fin m`: Coxeter length and inversions
(`TypeA.length_eq_invCount`), a normal form (`TypeA.braidEquiv_canWord_or_hasRepeat`), and
Matsumoto's theorem for `S_m` (`TypeA.braidEquiv_of_isReduced`), and factorisation through
minimal coset representatives of `S_n × S_{n'}` with additivity of length
(`TypeA.parabolicEquiv`, `TypeA.length_blockPerm_mul`).

### Symmetric polynomials

`Categorification.symmetricBasis`: over any commutative ring, `k[x_0, …, x_{n-1}]` is a free
module of rank `n!` over the symmetric polynomials, with basis the monomials `x^u`,
`u_a ≤ a` (`finrank_symmetric`); more generally, free of rank `|G|` over the invariants of a
label-preserving group `G` (`Categorification.finrank_labelInvariants`).

### Divided differences

`Categorification.ddiff`: divided difference operators on `MvPolynomial` over any commutative
ring, with the nilHecke relations (`ddiff_spec`, `ddiff_ddiff`, `ddiff_mul`, `ddiff_braid`).

## Maintenance

Deferred cleanup items: [naming cleanup](docs/naming-cleanup.md) (theorem numbers and process
adjectives in declaration and module names), to be done in one pass.

## Building

Requires [elan](https://github.com/leanprover/elan). Toolchain
`leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1` and Mathlib `v4.34.1` are pinned.
The default branch is `master`. The project dependencies
`string-diagrams-lean`, `lie-lean` and `dg-lean` are pinned to Git revisions in `lakefile.lean` and
`lake-manifest.json`. Warnings are errors; the default build covers every
`Categorification` submodule.

```sh
lake exe cache get
lake build
```
